No Slack, ITSM or dashboard router exists. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-08, D-D) to register this and build nothing now; the wording is reconciled whenever the intent is next revisited. | Low |
| **GAP-108** | **The Triage Rule Has Two Blunt Edges** | §6.15 (Inspector): the `/api/v1/triage` incident for any failed run with no missing secret and no "timeout" in its error gets category `CRASH_LOOP`, whether or not it looped, and severity is `CRITICAL` only when the error text contains "OOM". TSK-138 moves the rule and pins today's output in a test; changing either is new behavior and its own later task. | Low |
| **GAP-109** | **The Egress Registers Its Own Metrics Instruments** | §6.15 (Inspector, Gatekeeper, Executive, Treasurer): `gatekeeper-egress.ts` creates its request, latency, token and cost instruments inline from the meter `initTelemetry` gives it. The request count and latency are the Gatekeeper's, the tokens are the Executive's and the cost is the Treasurer's, so they stay with their owners; only the OpenTelemetry setup (`packages/telemetry`) is the Inspector's. Resolved when the Executive and Gatekeeper steps (roadmap steps 6 and 8) break up the file. | Low |
| **GAP-110** | **FinOps Visibility Gaps: Cloud Spend, Compute Inventory and Factory Budget Governance** | §6.15 (Treasurer): The factory metered model spend per agent, but cloud infrastructure spend and container compute inventory were not unified into factory reporting, forcing agents to contemplate direct AWS access. The Treasurer must own cloud infrastructure spend, compute inventory reporting, and factory budget governance via control-plane API endpoints, so agents never hold direct AWS credentials. | High |
| **GAP-111** | **A `FactoryState` Built by Hand Fails Only at Runtime Without an Inspector** | §6.15 (Inspector): `FactoryState.inspector` is required (TSK-139), but tests build the state as an object cast to `FactoryState` and `tsc` does not type-check tests, so a test that touches the progress or event routes without one fails at run time. The three tests that needed it were found by running them; a shared test-state helper would make the next one hard to miss. Raised in the review of PR #115. | Low |
| **GAP-112** | **Nothing Calls `Inspector.stop()` on Shutdown** | §6.15 (Inspector): `createInspector().stop()` ends every bus it attached, but the control plane's shutdown path never calls it. Harmless for a process-lifetime object; the contract is only complete when shutdown calls it. Raised in the review of PR #115. | Low |
| **GAP-113** | **Two Copies of the Bearer-Token Reader** | §6.15 (Inspector, Bouncer): `control-plane/src/progress-routes.ts` keeps a local `bearer()` that `app.ts` also exports as `bearerOf` (made public by #113). Moved as it was because the extraction changes no behavior. Raised in the reviews of PRs #114 and #115. | Low |
| **GAP-114** | **The Registrar Lists a `system-doctor` Built-in Agent With No Code Behind It** | §6.15 (Registrar, Inspector): `registrar/src/catalog.ts` lists a built-in agent with `memoryPrefix: 'system-doctor'`, an image name `factory-triage:latest` that nothing builds, `dir: '/app/packages/triage'` (a folder removed under D-B), a `/hooks/triage` trigger nothing serves, and an `/api/v1/triage` trigger that is the Inspector's incident route. It appears in the fleet as a working built-in agent. Not part of the Inspector extraction; resolved by building the alert router (GAP-107) or removing the entry. | Low |
| **GAP-115** | **Cartridge Memory Archetype Schema & Bounds Declaration** | Cartridges previously had no formal contract representation for memory behavior, relying on implicit conventions. Cartridges must declare their memory archetype (`ephemeral`, `episodic`, `workspace`) and bounding parameters (`retentionDays`, `maxMessages`, `maxChars`, optional `prefix`) in `cartridge.yaml` (or `memory.yaml`), validated strictly by `packages/contract` with standard-library reference implementations in `agents/examples/starter-python`. | Medium |
| **GAP-116** | **The Factory Does Not Read the Memory Declaration, and a Run's Memory Scope Comes From Its Caller** | §6.6, A2, E8, §6.15 (Secretary's hydration; Landlord's run start; found in the post-merge review of PR #118): (1) `cartridge.yaml` declares `memory.archetype`, `retentionDays`, `maxMessages`, `maxChars` and `persistence.enabled`, and `packages/contract` validates them (GAP-115), but nothing in the control plane or the shim reads any of them: only `persistence.prefix` is used, fixed when the agent is deployed, and the shim pulls and pushes whenever a memory store is configured. An "ephemeral" agent is ephemeral only because its own code writes no files. (2) The user and project an agent partitions memory by (`authorId`, `project_id` in the starter) come from the run's `input`, which the caller of the wake wrote (GAP-092); the Factory passes the agent no verified scope. (3) One storage prefix and one `$MEMORY_DIR` serve every project of a workspace agent, so a run pulls and pushes every project's files, and runs of one agent still execute one at a time. Per-run or per-scope hydration, a verified scope on the run, and what the Factory does with each archetype are design decisions for Dale and belong with the memory contract of the Secretary and the Landlord; this gap records the problem and is not a decision. | Medium |
| **GAP-117** | **The Model-Service Member Is Renamed the Executive** | §6.15, SV1: Dale chose the name Executive for the model-service member (chat, 2026-10-09): the cast member that gives an agent its access to a model, and so its executive function. The name appears in §6.15, the cast list, `FACTORY_SERVICE_NAMES`/`FACTORY_SERVICES`, `README.md`, the roadmap document and comments; SV1's cast test requires the document and the code to agree. Dale asked that no trace of the old name remain, so it is replaced everywhere, in earlier rows and the roadmap document's filename too. Name only; the role, its files and the extraction (roadmap step 6, not yet planned) are unchanged. | Low |
| **GAP-118** | **User & Project Budgets: Actor-Level Ceilings for Type 3 Workspace Agents** | §6.15 item 7, §6.12: Currently budgets are configured only per agent (`AgentPolicy.budgetUsd`). On Type 3 workspace agents where multiple operators collaborate, a single heavy operator exhausts the agent budget and starves all other users. Spend on the Factory Ledger lacks actor attribution. Needs design confirmation on actor-level budget ceilings via Bouncer identity links and Treasurer actor spend aggregation, followed by project ceilings scoped via GAP-116. | Medium |
| **GAP-119** | **The Executive's Model Service Is Inline in `gatekeeper-egress` and Shares Its Main File** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Executive, Gatekeeper), M1, M2, M4, E5, E7: the Executive's code was `models.ts` (catalog, request validation, the Bedrock Converse adapter, `ModelUpstreamError`), `meter.ts` (`SseMeter`, `usageFromJson`, `Provider`, `Usage`) and `sigv4.ts` in `packages/gatekeeper-egress/src`, with its model-policy gate (`modelDenial`) and its factory model API handler (`handleModels`) inline in `gatekeeper-egress.ts`, which the Gatekeeper also owned (`SPLIT_FILES`). There was no `packages/executive`, no typed contract for the policy gate or the call, and no guard against a second copy. Only the egress and its tests imported this code. Resolved by the Executive flow work stream (TSK-148 to TSK-155). The model-selection behavior in `docs/executive-roadmap.md` is not part of this gap; it is planned after the extraction. **Resolved 2026-10-10** by TSK-147 to TSK-155 (the plan in #125, the code in #128, #132 and #134); what stays outside is GAP-130. | Medium |
| **GAP-120** | **Skills Have No Visibility or Owner (Public vs Private)** | SK1, SK3, E8: a registered skill has no `visibility` and no owner agent. A skill that belongs to one agent cannot be hidden from the others, cannot auto-adopt for its owner on approval, and every skill in the catalog looks adoptable by everyone. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-09) the terms are public and private, not "shared"; a private skill still needs admin approval of each version, then adopts for its owner only. | High |
| **GAP-121** | **Skill Manifests Declare No Actions, so HITL Cannot Be Authored per Skill** | E9, E11, GAP-070: `skill.yaml` (`packages/contract/src/skill.ts`) declares `requires` only. The skill author cannot list actions (method and path) or mark each held (HITL) or autonomous, so the approval screen cannot show them and the grant derivation has nothing to read. **Audit 2026-10-19 (TSK-251): RESOLVED for the manifest (TSK-156).** `packages/contract/src/skill.ts` takes `actions[{ id, route, method, path, hold }]` with `hold` required and no default; `skill.test.ts` passes (8 tests). The SK2 coverage row still says "Manifest has no actions" and is stale (listed for the user). Enforcement of a hold remains GAP-070. | High |
| **GAP-122** | **No Adoption: Approved Skills Cannot Be Adopted, and Nothing Records Who Uses What** | SK3, SK5: `skills: []` in all ten live configuration records (checked 2026-10-09) while `tavily-search` 0.1.0, `print` 0.1.1, `discord-progress` 0.1.1 and `factory-schedules` 0.1.1 are approved. No route writes the `skills` list (`config-store.ts` only carries it forward), approval and adoption are one act in practice (the `tavily-search` approval reason names agents), and the registry cannot answer which agents use a skill. Higgins carries its own copies of `tavily-search` and `print` in `SM-higgins/skills`. | Critical |
| **GAP-123** | **A Configuration Change Does Not Rebuild or Redeploy the Agent** | SK4, L3, L4: `factory-agent-builder` builds the agent's own commit only. Nothing builds the agent's source plus adopted skills at their pinned commits, tags by configuration hash, or redeploys when the configuration changes, so adoption cannot take effect without an agent repo change. | High |
| **GAP-124** | **The Console and API Cannot Show Skill Adopters or Manage Adoption** | SK3, SK5, A2: no endpoint lists adopters per skill or skills per agent, and the console has no adopters panel, no agent Skills tab, no retire action and no adoption approval. An admin cannot see which agents use which skill or retire one. | High |
| **GAP-125** | **The Configuration API Shows a Private Skill to Every Viewer** | SK7, A2: a private skill is visible only to admins and to the owners of its owner agent, and the skills catalog and `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/skills` now enforce that (TSK-158). But `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/config`, its history and its versions return the whole configuration record, including `skills` and `skillAdoptions`, to any viewer (`config.read`), so a viewer learns that an agent adopted a private skill and who approved it. Found 2026-10-10 while writing TSK-158. Fix: filter private skills out of those responses for a viewer who may not see them, or move the skills list to a privilege an owner and admin hold. | Medium |
| **GAP-126** | **Model Choice Is Declared in Code, and the Factory Refuses a Model Instead of Serving the Owner's Choice** | §6.9 M2, E7, SK2, §6.15 (Executive); found 2026-10-05 (the Donna incident, `docs/executive-roadmap.md`), ruled on by Dale (chat, 2026-10-10): model choice is configuration, not code; the owner picks the model, a backup and optionally a model per skill through the API and console; nothing in code names a model; no preferred-model option. The code differs in four ways. (1) `modelDenial` answers 403 `model_not_allowed` to a call whose model is not on the allow-list; nothing serves the owner's choice instead. (2) The policy holds only an allow-list (`models`): no selected model, no backup, no per-skill model. (3) Model names are written in code and metadata: the cartridge's `model` and `models` (`contract/src/schema.ts`), the registrar's `requestedModels`, the registration route reading `cartridge.model` (`app.ts`), the skill manifest's `requires.models` (`contract/src/skill.ts`) and the missing-models check in `control-plane/src/skills.ts`. (4) Cartridges carry their own failback (SM-donna's `DEFAULT_FALLBACK_MODEL`), which the owner cannot see or change. Resolved by the model configuration work stream (TSK-165 to TSK-170), after the extraction. | Medium |
| **GAP-127** | **The Perimeter Admits a Person Only by Factory Role or Ownership, so an Agent Role Alone Is Refused** | E12, A2, GAP-090: `authorizeIngress` asks for `agents.wake` (a message that starts a run) or `agents.converse` (a conversation), held only by a factory `operator` or `admin` or by the agent's owner. A person whose only grant is an agent role (Stephanie: `Family` on Donna) is refused at the door, so the only way to let her message Donna is a factory `operator` role, which reaches every agent. Dale (chat, 2026-10-10): roles grant humans access; a stranger's message must not be delivered; a person must be able to hold less than full access. Found 2026-10-10 reading `packages/bouncer/src/identity.ts`. | High |
| **GAP-128** | **Nothing in the Factory Enforces a Person's Agent Roles on What an Agent Reaches** | E12, E11, S1, A2: an agent's roles (`cartridge.yaml` `roles`) are evaluated only in the cartridge's own code (`rbac.py`). The gatekeeper-egress never asks who a run is for, so during Stephanie's run a call through a route the agent holds for its owner (Gmail) is forwarded if the agent makes it, through a bug or a steering prompt. The rule holds only while the agent behaves: it is a courtesy, not a boundary. The run already records its requester (`requestedBy`) and the egress already reads the run (`egress.run.read`); nothing connects them. **Audit 2026-10-19 (TSK-251): OPEN.** Roles are declared (TSK-172) and assignable (TSK-173), but the gatekeeper-egress has no role check (no `roles` or requester code under `packages/gatekeeper/src/egress`); `access-enforcement.e2e.test.ts` does not exist; E12 is `unchecked`; TSK-174 and TSK-176 are `PENDING`. | High |
| **GAP-129** | **The Console Cannot Assign Agent Roles, and the Factory Does Not Know What an Agent Declares** | E12, §6.5: `PUT /api/v1/identity-links/:provider/:id` accepts `agentRoles`, but the Identities screen has no field for them (it sets factory roles only). The agent record does not carry the cartridge's declared roles, so a role name is unchecked free text, and a cartridge's skill entry names its system in free text, not the routes it uses, so a role cannot be mapped to what the egress sees. | High |
| **GAP-130** | **What Stays in the Egress After the Executive's Extraction** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Executive, Gatekeeper, Treasurer, Auditor, Inspector): the Executive owns no file in `gatekeeper-egress/src` any more, but the Executive's work is still called from, and surrounded by, other members' code in `gatekeeper-egress.ts`: the `handleModels` and `handleLlm` handlers (where the run token, the budget standing, the throttle, the price check, the Auditor's ledger rows, the Inspector's metrics and traces, and the OpenAI-format response body meet a request); `recordUsage`; `modelPolicyOf`, which builds the Executive's plain input from the run's context; and the `DEFAULT_MODEL` constant with the `defaultModel` option, operations config that the Executive is handed. Dale's rule (the file-ownership rule in §6.15) puts them with the Gatekeeper, the same call the Inspector's extraction made for its handlers (GAP-104 to GAP-109). Two things will change them: the Gatekeeper's own extraction (roadmap step 8) and the model configuration work (TSK-166 changes `handleModels` to serve the owner's selection). Nothing is wrong today; this records why the Executive is extracted and the egress file is still large. The machine-readable map agrees with this gap: by the file-ownership rule these are the Gatekeeper's code (they call the Executive's contracts, which does not make the Executive a co-owner), so the Executive's `hostedIn` lists no egress package and `gatekeeper-egress.ts` is the Gatekeeper's alone. | Low |
| **GAP-131** | **Scheduled Runs Carry No Requester, so the Agent Cannot Tell Whose Authority They Have** | E12, E11, S1, A2 (GitHub issue #139): the Timekeeper fires a dynamic schedule through `createRun` with the actor `factory:scheduler` and no `caller` badge, so the cartridge's fail-closed RBAC resolves the run to an unknown guest. On 2026-10-10 Donna refused the owner's own morning debrief ("I won't share Dale's emails with you"). A schedule also records nobody: when a role holder asks an agent to schedule work, it would have to run with someone's authority, and none is recorded, so the only safe reading of a scheduled run is none. A fix that made every scheduled run owner-level would let a `Family` member schedule a read of the owner's email. | High |
| **GAP-132** | **The Landlord's Compute Lifecycle Is Inline in `app.ts` and `control-plane`** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Landlord), L1 to L6: the Landlord's code is `runs.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `runtime-docker.ts`, `runtime-ecs.ts`, `callbacks.ts`, `queues.ts`, `aws/*` and `gcp/*` (image build, IAM, task and job registration) in `packages/control-plane/src`, and, inline in `app.ts` (2,340 lines at `148984e`, shared by every member), the run lifecycle: `createRun`, `finishRun`, `blockRun`, `unblockRun`, `activeRun`, `scheduleTimeout`, the mailbox (`deliverToMailbox`), `applyKillSwitch`, `haltUnhealthy`, `checkHealth`, `reconcileRuns` and `resetAgent`. There is no `packages/landlord`, no typed contract, and no guard against a second copy. `queues.ts` imports `createRun` back from `app.ts`, a cycle (as GAP-097 was for the Timekeeper). `runs.ts` imports the Bouncer, `runtime.ts` imports the Registrar and the Secretary, and `aws/codebuild.ts` is also the Registrar's (`split`). About 45 files import these modules, most of them e2e tests. The file map in `FACTORY_SERVICES.landlord.owns` also lists `apply.ts` and all of `app.ts`, which the extraction must check against the code. Resolved by the Landlord flow work stream (TSK-179 to TSK-186). **Resolved 2026-10-11** by TSK-178 to TSK-186 (the plan in #143, the code in #144 to #148 and the last PR); what stays outside is GAP-133. | Medium |
| **GAP-133** | **What Stays in `control-plane` After the Landlord's Extraction** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Landlord, Bouncer, Gatekeeper, Registrar, Treasurer, Inspector): `packages/landlord` holds the run lifecycle, the run store, the queue pollers, the runtime launchers, callback delivery and the AWS and GCP adapters. Still in `control-plane`, by decision and not oversight: (1) the HTTP handlers that call the Landlord (`handleMcp`, the wake, run, mailbox and kill-switch routes in `app.ts`, which the Landlord shares with the Bouncer, Registrar, Inspector and Auditor, so `app.ts` stays `split`), because that is where the Bouncer's privileges and the Gatekeeper's run-token rule meet a request, as for the Bouncer, Timekeeper and Inspector (GAP-089, GAP-104); (2) `FactoryState`, which the Landlord reads through its structural `LandlordState` (24 fields) and the policy store through a two-method port (`LandlordPolicies`); (3) `apply.ts`, the Landlord's side of applying a configuration, which also uses the Registrar's records and stays until the Registrar's version flow (`docs/registrar-roadmap.md`); (4) `unblockAffordableRuns`, `handoffRefusal` and `enrichAgent` in `app.ts`, which are the Treasurer's budget and the console's view and call the Landlord. Not moved and worth a decision: the lifecycle names two cartridges by id (`med-doc` for crashes, `finops-officer` for budget alerts, in `finishRun` and `routeEvents`); that is routing configuration in Factory code and belongs in an event-route setting or a cartridge's own manifest (the Donna Test: the Factory routes the event, the cartridge decides what to do with it). A comment in `packages/conformance/src/inspector.test.ts` still names `control-plane/src/gcp/cloudbuild.ts`. Resolved when each handler group moves with its member and `app.ts` stops being split. | Medium |
| **GAP-134** | **The Gatekeeper Is in Four Packages and Owns Code in `control-plane`** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Gatekeeper), A1, A2, E1 to E6, S1: the Gatekeeper is `packages/gatekeeper-ingress`, `packages/gatekeeper-egress` and `packages/auth`, and `hostedIn` also lists `packages/control-plane` for three pieces of its code there: `policy.ts` (the policy store and its validation), `identity-links.ts` (the Discord-to-person links, authentication data) and the run-token rule, `authenticateRun` inline in `app.ts`. `gatekeeper-egress` also holds `signin-links.ts`, which is the Keymaster's (`FACTORY_SERVICES.keymaster.owns`), so it is still a shared package (`SHARED_PACKAGES`). There is no `packages/gatekeeper`, no typed contract for verifying a run token, and no guard against a second copy. Roadmap step 8 says egress "is the perimeter" once the Executive, Treasurer and Inspector are out; that is now true (TSK-153), so this is the last extraction. Resolved by the Gatekeeper flow work stream (TSK-187 to TSK-194). **Resolved 2026-10-12** by TSK-187 to TSK-194 (the plan in #150, the code in #152, #153, #154, #155, #158 and #160, and the last PR); what stays outside is GAP-133, GAP-136 and GAP-137, and the extraction found GAP-138 and GAP-139. | Medium |
| **GAP-135** | **The Cast Has No Public Documentation Site That Stays True to the Code** | §6.15, SV1, §6.7: Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-11) that the company site, `https://beercanlabs.ai` (the `.com` redirects to it), is built from this repository and published automatically when changes merge to `main`: a home page on the Agent Factory's purpose, a staff page for the eleven cast members with each one's key product flows, a page for every contract between them, and one infrastructure blueprint per provider (AWS and GCP) mapping each member to the service that serves it. The site documents **intent**, not execution (Dale, chat, 2026-10-11): no package names, build or extraction status, gap or task numbers, or Terraform files. Hand-written pages drift, so `site/check.mjs` fails when the cast differs from `FACTORY_SERVICE_NAMES`, when a contract's source file does not exist, when a contract is used by no flow, when a blueprint lacks a row for a member, when a built link is broken, or when the content carries execution detail. The site is static (no script, no new interface, `KPF.md` is untouched); it is hosted by GitHub Pages and the domain is on Cloudflare. CLAUDE.md §1 keeps company deployment details out of this repository: the site holds public documentation only (no account ids, credentials or internal scripts); the DNS records and the Pages setting live in the providers' settings, not here. | Low |
| **GAP-136** | **The Identity-Link Store Is in the Bouncer's Package, but §6.15 Calls It the Gatekeeper's Data** | SV1, §6.15 (Gatekeeper, Bouncer), A2: the Gatekeeper entry says the identity links an admin sets (a Discord user to a person) are its data. In the code `IdentityLinkStore`, `parseActor`, `PROVIDERS` and `PRINCIPAL_ACTOR` are in `packages/bouncer` (the Bouncer flow, GAP-088), and `control-plane/src/identity-links.ts` is only the `/api/v1/identity-links` handler plus re-exports of them, which stays in `control-plane` (GAP-133's reasoning). The Gatekeeper plan (TSK-190) assumed the store was in `control-plane`; it was not, so nothing moved. **Resolved 2026-10-12** (Dale, chat): the identity links are the Bouncer's. The Gatekeeper's job is to verify a credential at the door (A1, A2); a link only says which identities are one person, and exists to decide what that person may do. The store stays in `packages/bouncer`, the machine-readable map already gave the handler to the Bouncer, and the §6.15 text is corrected (TSK-196). No code moved. | Low |
| **GAP-137** | **The Keymaster Verifies a Run Token Itself** (part 1 resolved 2026-10-12; part 2 open) | SV2, §6.15 (Gatekeeper, Keymaster), A1: `packages/keymaster/src/keymaster.ts` `checkout` calls `RunTokens.verify` and then decides for itself whether the run is live (its own status codes, 401, 404 and 409, and a hard-coded list of ended run states), where the Gatekeeper's `verifyRun` (TSK-192) is the one place that answers it. It cannot call the contract: `packages/gatekeeper` depends on `packages/keymaster` (the sign-in link rule, TSK-191), so the reverse would be a cycle. The Gatekeeper guard (`packages/conformance/src/gatekeeper.test.ts`) names this file as its one exception, and fails when the file stops doing it. **Decided 2026-10-12 (Dale, chat), part 1 only:** the Keymaster does not verify run tokens; the caller does. Found while planning: the only production caller, the checkout route in `app.ts`, never passes `runToken`, so the Keymaster's token check (`checkout` step 1) is dead code in production, and the route already verifies the caller (`authenticateRun` on the agent path, the `egress.keymaster.checkout` privilege on the egress path). **Part 1 resolved 2026-10-12** by TSK-204, which removed the check, the `runToken` parameter, the `runTokens` option, the Keymaster's `factory-auth` dependency and the guard test's named exception. **Part 2, left open:** `checkout` step 2 (`getRun`, with its own hard-coded list of ended run states) is a second copy of what `verifyRun` answers; on the egress path nothing in the control plane checks that the run is live, so it is not removed until it is confirmed that the egress does at its own door. | Low |
| **GAP-138** | **The Egress Is One Function in One File** | §6.15 (Gatekeeper): `packages/gatekeeper/src/egress/gatekeeper-egress.ts` is about 1,000 lines and `createGatekeeperEgress` is one closure that holds every route (the model route, the connection token, the held requests, the progress emitter, the credential injection and the generic proxy). The extraction moved it whole, by decision (Dale, chat, 2026-10-11: confirmed default), because splitting it is not a move. Worth doing when the egress next changes; it does not block anything. No code until asked. | Low |
| **GAP-139** | **The Spend Reader Is Written Twice** | §6.15 (Treasurer, Gatekeeper), E7: `GET /api/v1/spend` in `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts` and `spendReader` in `packages/control-plane/src/spend.ts` both decide who may read spend (a live run whose admin-set policy grants `factory-spend`, or a principal with `spend.read`). Found while wiring `verifyRun` (TSK-192): both call the contract, but the rest of the logic is duplicated, so one can drift from the other. Not part of the Gatekeeper extraction (it is the Treasurer's route); left as two. No code until asked. | Low |
| **GAP-140** | **The Secretary Is an Artificially Decoupled Cast Member for Container State Hydration** | SV1, SV2, §6.6, §6.15: The Secretary's only responsibility is syncing local SQLite files between object storage and `$MEMORY_DIR` on container wake/sleep (`packages/hydrate`). In container orchestration, entrypoint shimming, volume hydration, process supervision, and signal forwarding are runtime lifecycle responsibilities. Having a dedicated Cast member and a cross-service contract (`secretary-mind`) between the Landlord and Secretary is unnecessary architectural overhead. Resolved by folding Secretary into Landlord, making hydration an internal Landlord runtime implementation detail, and updating the Cast to 10 canonical services (TSK-196). | Low |
| **GAP-141** | **A Person Waiting in Discord Sees Dead Silence During a Long Turn** | P1, §6.5, E3, §6.15 (Gatekeeper): Discord expires the typing indicator after about 10 s, so a turn that calls tools or runs a long inference (15 to 45 s) looks like a crashed agent. The Gatekeeper already holds the Discord connection (ingress) and already sees every outbound call (egress progress events, §6.5, metadata only). Resolved by a work-leased typing heartbeat in `gatekeeper-ingress` fed by the existing progress stream: the control plane relays live-run progress to the existing internal ingress API (`POST /api/v1/activity`, the same bearer-token surface and caller as `POST /api/v1/presence`, so no new interface kind, KPF 5). The ingress shows `Tool: <route>...` or `Thinking...`, and goes silent when the agent replies or no activity arrives for the lease (30 s, `GATEKEEPER_INGRESS_TURN_LEASE_MS`). Not a Cartridge concern (Donna Test): it is presence, which the Factory owns. Review of PR #159 found and fixed (TSK-197): a status left on the bot by late events, a lease that was not enforced the same way in the standby path, duplicated heartbeat code, an unbounded activity request, unordered relay, the `tool` field that nothing populated (progress names the route, never the tool, E3), an untested lease and an unregistered change. Open by design: activity names an agent, not a channel, so it keeps every open turn of that agent alive; a reply (first message from the bot) ends the turn. | Medium |
| **GAP-142** | **Service Taxonomy Cast Names Diverge From Settled Titles (Landlord → Property Manager, Treasurer → Accountant)** | SV1, SV2, §6.15: Dale directed that Landlord be renamed to Property Manager (`property-manager`) and Treasurer be renamed to Accountant (`accountant`) across the Cast, code, package names (`packages/property-manager`), contracts, and documentation. Updating the canonical identifiers unifies the cast titles with the underlying service keys and package hierarchy. | Low |
| **GAP-143** | **Cast Taxonomy Rename Polish: Invariant Citations, Lingering References, and Legacy Aliases** | SV1, SV2, L1–L6, §6.15: Following the rename of Landlord to Property Manager and Treasurer to Accountant (GAP-142, TSK-198), code review identified polish items: restore L1–L6 invariant citations (PM1–PM6 do not exist in §6), sweep lingering [treasurer] log prefixes and cast.json role texts, fix internal accountantCredentials call sites, remove dead compatibility aliases, and tighten cast parser length checks. | Low |
| **GAP-144** | **Cast Member Portrait Uncanny Valley Styling (Creepy Human Teeth & Gums on Robot Faceplate)** | SV1, §6.15: The 10 cast portraits in `site/static/cast/*.png` generated by Grok contained human teeth and pink mouth cavities on the robot chassis, producing an uncanny/creepy aesthetic. Resolved by updating the 10 portraits with a clean, antialiased mechanical robot smile seam, eliminating human mouth features while preserving 2.5D vector illustration style, character props, lighting, and 512x512 RGBA transparent format (TSK-200). | Low |
| **GAP-145** | **Transient `aws s3 sync` Failure on Startup Leaves Configuration Store Unavailable (`state.configs` Undefined)** | §6.14 SK3, §6.13 R1, §6.12 A2: `S3ConfigBackend.loadAll()` in `packages/registrar/src/config-store.ts` executes a single `aws s3 sync` call when the Control Plane starts. If the Fargate task metadata credential endpoint or S3 network path experiences a transient failure during container cold boot, `VersionedConfigStore.open(backend)` throws on the first attempt and `state.configs` remains `undefined` for the lifetime of the Control Plane process—silently dropping agent `owners` resolution (`isOwner: false`) and `/configs` routes until restart. Resolved by adding bounded retry with exponential backoff (default 3 attempts, fresh scratch directory per attempt) to `S3ConfigBackend.loadAll()` (TSK-201). | High |
| **GAP-146** | **Decoupled Tasks & Schedules: Archetype-Aware Execution & Multi-User Timekeeping** | §6.15 Timekeeper, E12, §6.6: Currently `ScheduledAction` bundles the task definition, temporal schedule, executor, and commissioner into a single flat model tied 1:1 to an individual human requester. On Type 3 workspace agents and Type 2 collaborative agents, this causes project automation to silently fail when an engineer departs, prevents peer collaborators from managing shared tasks, and misattributes project spend. Needs architectural decoupling into Task (definition and scope), Schedule (temporal rules), Executor (agent cartridge), and Commissioner (creator provenance vs scope execution authority). | Medium |
| **GAP-147** | **GCP Landing Zone & Kernel Adapters Lag Behind AWS Parity** | §6.2–§6.15 (E1–E12, S1, L1–L6, M1–M4, K1–K5, A1–A2, R1, SK1–SK5, LG1, SV1–SV2) & GAP-010: While the AWS landing zone and adapters evolved through TSK-018–TSK-204, the GCP pattern (`landing-zones/gcp` and GCP adapters across the kernel) was left behind: `runtime-cloudrun.ts` is missing (`FACTORY_RUNTIME=cloudrun` falls through to `memoryRuntime`, GAP-010); `gcp/deploy.ts` throws `not_supported`; `gcp/cloudbuild.ts` lacks L3 admission gates (secret scan, test suite, SK4 skill layer composition, source token host guard); `gcp/cloudrun.ts` and `gcp/iam.ts` lack `agentEnvironment`, `agentContainerSecrets` (S1), and isolated `agents` VPC attachment; `secrets-bind` `gcpSecretManagerProvider` only implements `get` (missing `put`, `has`, `present` and secret prefixing) and shells out to `gcloud` which is not installed in the Alpine runtime images (`hydrate`, `ledger`, and `secrets-bind` all fail in-container without native GCP REST API + Metadata Server/ADC support); `registrar/src/config-store.ts` lacks `GcsConfigBackend` (`gs://`/`gcs://`, SK3); `executive/src/models.ts` only registers `bedrock-converse` (missing Vertex AI and direct API-key model adapters, M1–M3); `control-plane` lacks `cloudBuildSkillChecker` (SK1) and `gcp/accountant.ts` (`GcpComputeSource` + BigQuery `GcpCostSource`, §6.15); `inspector/src/events.ts` lacks a `pubsub:` sink; and `landing-zones/gcp` lacks `bootstrap/main.tf` (WIF + state bucket), `lb.tf` (Cloud Armor + External ALB + Managed SSL), the `gatekeeper-ingress` and `console` Cloud Run services, `/data` GCS state volume mount (R1), and local `/tmp/ledger.jsonl` hydration from WORM GCS checkpoints (LG1). **Resolved 2026-10-15 by TSK-205.** | Critical |
| **GAP-148** | **GCP parity review defects** | S1, L3, LG2: PR #173 admission and skill-check steps used `python:3.12` without git or Node; static agent IAM granted the unprefixed secret; a failed GCS ledger hydrate opened an empty ledger; BigQuery `jobs.query` was treated as finished when `jobComplete` was false; gatekeeper-egress had project-wide `secretAccessor`. **Resolved 2026-10-15 by TSK-206.** | High |
| **GAP-160** | **No House Staff and No Skill Training Account** | SK1, E5, S1, L2, L5, §6.15: Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-16) that the factory ships an optional Personal Trainer, one house character, not an eleventh cast member. It trains a skill (public or private), guides the builder through a baseline, scores pinned trials, and recommends a model. It does not rewrite the skill and it does not deploy (L5). Training spend posts to a skill account that starts empty; adopting or using the skill still posts to the agent's budget. House staff live in `agents/staff` and are absent from the baked catalog. The conversation API is the only door. **Resolved 2026-10-16** by TSK-220. | Medium |
| **GAP-161** | **Action Provenance & Multi-Surface Hallucination Bypass** | E9, E1, M1, §6.16: Agents can claim state-altering external actions (e.g., posting to X, pushing git commits) via raw LLM text hallucination without making real tool calls or routing through gatekeeper-egress. The Executive dropped `tools` on Bedrock Converse calls, forcing models to simulate tools in raw text. The platform lacked tool-syntax leak rejection at gatekeeper-egress and action provenance verification at the Turn Boundary, allowing unverified external action claims to pass through to outbound delivery surfaces (Discord, console, etc.) without HITL approval or ledgered receipts. | Critical |
| **GAP-162** | **Static Model Catalog & Disconnected Model Onboarding (Cast-Centric Model Governance)** | §6.9 M1–M4, §6.8 E10, §6.11 K1–K5, §6.3 E5/E7, §6.4: The factory model catalog previously relied exclusively on static infrastructure environment variables (`FACTORY_MODEL_CATALOG`), while agents, skills, and systems were dynamically versioned and admitted through the Registrar. Model vendors (OpenAI, Anthropic, xAI, Google, Bedrock) serve multiple models under shared endpoints and API keys, matching the System Definition archetype (E10). Operators lacked dynamic vendor registration, on-demand model discovery via the Executive, Accountant pricing management via REST and MCP, and a Console experience structured around the Cast of Characters. | High |
| **GAP-163** | **Dynamic Model Catalog Sync Authz, Merging Fallback, Discovery SSRF, and Retirement Policy Invariants (PR #178 Review Findings)** | §6.9 M1–M4, §6.3 E5/E7, §6.11 K1–K5, §6.12 A1/A2, S1: PR #178 code review identified 5 defects in dynamic model onboarding: (1) internal catalog sync endpoint `/api/v1/models/internal/catalog` required `models.read` which `gatekeeper-egress` does not hold (needs `egress.models.read`); (2) `gatekeeper-egress` merged remote catalog with `{ ...opts.modelCatalog, ...remote }`, preventing retirement of bootstrap models; (3) template adapters used template enum names rather than completion adapter keys, endpoints were dropped, keys were submitted to unauthorized secret names, and model name collisions silently overwritten; (4) `/api/v1/models/scan` accepted raw caller-supplied `upstream` and `apiKey` returning upstream response text (authenticated SSRF); (5) retirement stripped policies before `retire()` succeeded and empty policy model arrays locked agents out of M2 default models. | High |
| **GAP-164** | **Vendor model discovery reads a gatekeeper-held key in the control plane** | S1, E5, K5, M1: `POST /api/v1/models/scan` (and MCP `scan_vendor_models`) calls `bindSecrets` for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and the other held provider keys, then `fetch`es the vendor from the control plane. The control plane is denied `GetSecretValue` on those keys, so the scan goes out with no key and returns HTTP 502 `discovery_failed`. The operator sees that as a 502 while saving an Anthropic key. The key must stay in the gatekeeper-egress, which already holds it and is the only process allowed to call the provider. | High |
| **GAP-165** | **Landing Zone Network Topology Missing Control-Plane to Gatekeeper-Egress Discovery Rule** | E5, S1, K5, §6.6: GAP-164 / TSK-225 routed AI vendor model discovery from the control plane to `gatekeeper-egress` (`POST /internal/vendor-models`) to preserve S1 / K5 (control plane never reads provider keys). However, the reference AWS ECS landing zone (`landing-zones/aws/network.tf`) only permitted inbound TCP 8081 on `gatekeeper-egress` from `agents` (`gw_from_ag`), omitting an ingress rule for `control-plane` (`gw_from_cp`). In micro-segmented networks with security groups, TCP SYN packets were silently dropped, causing discovery requests to hang and return HTTP 502 Bad Gateway to the operator during provider onboarding. Additionally, `landing-zones/PATTERNS.md` and `landing-zones/aws/README.md` lacked a canonical Service Traffic & Firewall Matrix defining required cross-service network edges for all landing zone implementations. | High |
| **GAP-166** | **Property Manager Run Model Injection and Pinning Semantics** | §6.9 M2, §6.3.1 E7, §6.15 (Property Manager, Gatekeeper Egress): On agent wake (Discord, webhook, cron, manual), the Property Manager must inject `FACTORY_MODEL` into container environment overrides (`runEnv`) so cartridges do not hardcode model names or fallbacks. However, `run.model` denotes a caller-specified hard run pin enforced by Gatekeeper Egress (`pinnedModel`). Waking with a policy default model must not set `run.model` (which causes Gatekeeper Egress to reject secondary models on multi-model policy allowlists with HTTP 403 `model_pinned`). `run.model` must remain unset unless the caller explicitly requested a pinned model, while `FACTORY_MODEL` is always delivered into the container environment. | High |
| **GAP-167** | **Platform credential names are a landing-zone list** | S1, K1, K5, K5.1, E5, E10, M3: Keymaster accepts a platform credential only when its name is in `FACTORY_GATEKEEPER_EGRESS_HELD_SECRETS`, set from `provider_secret_names` at deploy. The console submits the template name (`GEMINI_API_KEY`, or `OPENAI_API_KEY` again for a custom vendor). A new vendor cannot be stored. The approved system's `credential.secret` is the only list. | High |
| **GAP-168** | **Factory default model cannot be set or updated dynamically** | §6.9 M2, §6.9 M3, §6.10 LG1, §6.15: The factory default model granted to agents with unpinned or unspecified model policies is hardcoded to `claude-haiku-4-5` or configured at boot time via `FACTORY_DEFAULT_MODEL`. There is no mechanism to set or update the default model at runtime via REST API or MCP, `ModelStore` does not persist default model configuration, Gatekeeper Egress does not update active default model on dynamic catalog refresh, and the Console lacks controls to promote an admitted model to factory default. | Medium |
| **GAP-169** | **Model completions read any secret a catalog entry names** | S1, K5, M3, E5; recorded decision GAP-167/TSK-228 (a platform credential is an approved system's `credential.secret`; vendor discovery may read these and no others): PR #183 lets a catalog entry's `secret` choose which credential the gatekeeper-egress resolves for `/models/v1/chat/completions`, with no allowlist. Any secret the gatekeeper-egress can read (for example a route's bot token) could be sent to a model vendor or a catalog `endpoint`. Completions must read only platform credentials, as discovery does. | High |
| **GAP-170** | **Model calls wait on the control plane for the catalog** | §6.10 LG1 (recorded decision 2026-09-28: a control-plane deploy has a brief gap), §6.9 M3: PR #184 made `/models` requests await the catalog refresh whenever it is stale (every 10 s). A slow or hung control plane then adds up to 10 s (the client timeout) to a model call in each window, and a control-plane deploy stalls model calls. Route refresh keeps the last known systems for the same reason. The catalog should block only until it has loaded once. | Low |
| **GAP-171** | **Claude 5.5 calls fail: sampling parameters forwarded, and a failed first catalog load is treated as loaded** | §6.9 M1, M2, M3: (1) The gatekeeper-egress forwards an agent's `temperature`/`top_p` to Anthropic (direct, Vertex, Bedrock). Claude models from Opus 4.7 on (Opus 4.7/4.8/5/5.5, Sonnet 5/5.5, Haiku 5.5, Fable) reject them with 400 (`temperature` is deprecated for this model), so every `claude-haiku-5-5` / `claude-sonnet-5-5` completion fails (prod 2026-10-18: Donna, Switch). Agents name no model (M2), so they cannot know; translating to the provider's format is the gatekeeper-egress's job (M3). (2) GAP-170 waits for the catalog only while `lastCatalogRefresh === 0`, which a failed boot fetch also sets; after a deploy (control plane restarting) the first call sees only the landing-zone catalog and gets `model_not_offered`. | High |
| **GAP-172** | **CI takes ~6.5 min: serial jobs, images built twice, packages built twice** | §6.7 (CI green to merge; no skipped suite), TSK-033 (production images from a clean checkout): `compose-proof` waits for `test` (`needs: test`, ~1.5 min) though it uses none of its output; `package-lock.json` is out of sync (`control-plane` depends on `factory-executive` since #178), so `npm install` rewrites it and `compose-e2e.sh`'s `docker compose build` misses the layer cache and rebuilds every image (~81 s); the six production images build one at a time (~97 s); the root `build` script builds all 16 ordered packages a second time (~15 s per job). | Low |
| **GAP-173** | **The factory model API refuses tool results, so tool use breaks on the second turn** | §6.9 M1 (the factory model API is the OpenAI Chat Completions format), M3 (the gatekeeper-egress translates it into each provider's native format): #177 forwards `tools` and returns `tool_calls`, but `parseChatRequest` accepts only `system`/`user`/`assistant` text messages, so the follow-up carrying the tool result (`assistant` with `tool_calls`, then `role: "tool"` with `tool_call_id`) gets `400 unsupported_role`. Prod 2026-10-18 11:28Z: Rosie's `claude-haiku-5-5` call asked for `get_kitty_litter_status`; the next call was refused and she answered with her canned fallback. Every tool-using agent is affected. | High |
| **GAP-175** | **Model capabilities are scattered; forced tool choice breaks Claude Opus 5.5 / Sonnet 5.5** | §6.9 M1, M3; precedent GAP-171: agents name no model (M2), so the gatekeeper-egress must translate the factory model API into what each model accepts. GAP-171 added a single sampling check (`claudeAcceptsSampling`). Forced `tool_choice` (`any`/`tool`, sent for OpenAI `required` or a named function) is rejected with 400 by Claude Fable 5.1, Mythos 5.1, Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5, so an agent that retries with `tool_choice: 'required'` (Rosie's 422 retry; Donna runs `claude-sonnet-5-5`) fails. | High |
| **GAP-177** | **Console turns arrive empty or as separate runs** | §6.1 (the Console is the factory's front office; it calls the API), §6.3, §6.15: the Workbench's Live Mailbox and the Fleet wake dialog send `{input: {prompt}}` to `/agents/:id/wake`. Agents read `content`/`message` (`SM-rosie/worker.mjs:606`, `SM-template/agent.py:27`), so the turn arrives empty; and a wake for an agent with a live run queues a second run instead of reaching it (2026-10-18 04:23 run `80d35168` waited an hour). The terminal's opening lines are hard-coded and its "Mailbox delivered. Agent container warm window renewed." is printed by the console, not reported by the factory. | Medium |
| **GAP-174** | **No check ever exercises the model path agents use** | §6.7 (a deploy's definition of done includes one model call proven by its ledger row through a gatekeeper-egress provider route), §6.9 M1: the deploy's definition of done runs only `echo-agent`, which makes no model call; `llm-summarizer` (built but never run in prod) calls the provider-native `anthropic` route, not the factory model API (`/models/v1/chat/completions`) every agent uses; the Compose proof has no `models` route or catalog. On 2026-10-17/18 every prod model call failed (credentials, sampling parameters, tool results) while every deploy and CI run passed. | High |
| **GAP-176** | **A message to an agent with no live run never wakes it, and every deploy kills live runs** | §6.3, §6.15 (the Property Manager owns waking: `createRun` is the single entry point), §6.10 LG1 (control-plane deploys stop then start): ledger 2026-10-18 shows `CONVERSATION_HANDOFF` rows with no `runId` at 06:29:15, 11:01:42 and 11:03:53. Causes: (1) `/conversation` only fills the mailbox when no run is live; (2) ingress sets presence from `agent.state === 'WORKING'`, but `GET /api/v1/agents` reports `RUNNING` for an agent with a live run, so presence is inverted (live run → offline, wakes queue a second run; stale `WORKING` with no run → available, messages parked); (3) agent state changes in `startRun`/`finishRun`/`haltUnhealthy` are not persisted, so a stale `WORKING` survives a restart; (4) the control-plane target group drains 300 s by default, during which the stopped control plane, unreachable by agents, keeps running its health check and halts every live run (`HEARTBEAT_LOST` 06:15:47 → `RUN_FAILED` 06:16:44, error `factory scale-to-zero`; same at 11:39–11:41), and a new control plane would judge heartbeats older than its own start. Donna Test: factory. | High |
| **GAP-178** | **Answers given without tools reach the user unmarked** | §6.16 AP3 (the user's decision, 2026-10-18), AP1, AP2: the anti-hallucination change (#176) checks only claimed side effects (X/GitHub/LinkedIn URLs) and only on the run result, sent once at the end of a run, while agents post their replies to Discord themselves; a made-up answer that skipped its tools (or a canned fallback) reaches the user unmarked. AP3 replaces the user's original intent (reject and force a retry) with: warn the model up front, footnote no-tool answers, and an optional per-agent warn-once retry. | High |
| **GAP-179** | **Agent memory does not match the three archetypes the user decided** | §6.6 AM1–AM5 (the user's decision, 2026-10-18), GAP-116: the documented archetypes (ephemeral, episodic, workspace) differ from the decided ones (Purpose-Built, Personal Assistant, Team Member); `memory.archetype` is optional; conversations are keyed by channel and pruned after 14 days; the factory hydrates the whole memory directory on every wake and does not read the declaration; console turns carry no identity; nothing lets the console read or resume a conversation. | High |
| **GAP-180** | **Shared project conversations: open decision** | §6.6 open decision (2026-10-18): how a Team Member agent records and reads conversations several people share (GitHub issues, Jira tickets, Discord threads), including whether every channel is mirrored the same way given that each channel delivers comments differently. Deferred by the user; no rule yet. | Medium |
| **GAP-181** | **Agents' requested model decides what is served, and the default model is in code** | §6.9 M2 (a model name a call carries does not decide what serves it; the user's decision 2026-10-18: agents never choose, the default is configuration), M3: the factory model API refuses a requested model outside the policy (`model_not_allowed`) or requires one (`model_required`) instead of serving the owner's selection, so a policy change reaches a warm run only after a restart (2026-10-18 00:45 `EGRESS_DENIED_MODEL_NOT_ALLOWED`); the default `claude-haiku-4-5` is hard-coded in the Property Manager, control plane, gatekeeper-egress and the AWS landing zone (GCP: `claude-sonnet-4-6`); retiring a model edits policies without `POLICY_UPDATED` or a configuration version. | High |
| **GAP-182** | **Vendor, key and admitted model are not tied together** | §6.9 M3 (provider keys come from the Keymaster's platform credentials), §6.11 K5, E5, S1; GAP-167, GAP-169: the user's flow (register the vendor with the Registrar, upload its key with the Keymaster, approve models) is three unconnected steps. Admission trusts the caller's `provider` and `endpoint` (Quick Admit writes the adapter type `openAiCompatible`, which no adapter serves; the MCP tool drops `endpoint`); an admitted model never carries its vendor's credential, so a custom OpenAI-compatible vendor's models are sent `OPENAI_API_KEY`; and nothing binds a key to its vendor's host, so a catalog `endpoint` could receive any platform credential (the GAP-169 residual). | High |
| **GAP-183** | **A stale SQLite journal in the mind store corrupts the agent's notebook** | E6 (mind sync is platform traffic, owned by the factory), AM3 (the store keeps what a push does not delete): object-store pushes never delete, so a write-ahead log uploaded while an agent's database was open stays in the store after SQLite removes it on a clean close. The next wake pulls the newer database with the old log; SQLite replays it and the database is malformed. Seen in prod 2026-10-18 19:24 UTC: an agent's notebook (18:46 database, 17:52 log) failed `database disk image is malformed` at startup and the crashing run pushed the corrupt file back. | High |
| **GAP-184** | **A reply built from data the agent's code fetched is footnoted as ungrounded** | §6.16 AP3.2: the gatekeeper-egress judged grounding only by `tool` messages in the model request. An agent whose code fetches data before the model call and gives it to the model as text (2026-10-19 00:01 UTC: four `google-health` calls, then a model answer with the step count) got `UNGROUNDED_REPLY` and `[no tools called]` although the factory had carried out the calls. Open beside it: tool messages the agent writes itself are trusted, so a fabricated tool result counts as grounding. | Medium |
| **GAP-185** | **The gap register has no status, so it no longer says which gaps are open** | TSK-042 (the independent audit), §2 step 3: the register lists 173 gaps with only a severity. Whether a gap is open is inferred from task rows whose status was not kept current (TSK-021 is `PENDING` though the factory model API is live; TSK-019 was `COMPLETED` while GAP-027 was untrue). A reader cannot tell the open gaps from the closed ones. Audit order: batch 1 egress and models (GAP-026, 041, 053, 070, 121, 128); later batches by area. | Medium |
| **GAP-186** | **Discord Ingress Presence Only Shows Ephemeral Status; In-Channel Progress and Token/Tool Summary Missing** | P1, §6.5, E3/S1, §6.15 (Gatekeeper): Users in Discord waiting for agent turns had only bot activity presence ("Tool: ...", "Thinking..."), which is easily missed or ephemeral and provides no in-channel feedback that the agent is working. Moving progress into the channel via an agent skill (`skill-discord-progress`) violates the Donna Test by coupling cartridge code to platform channel management. Gatekeeper Ingress owns the Discord channel interaction: it should create an in-channel progress message, throttle edits during tool/model calls, replace the progress message with a concise turn summary ("Thought for [time], called [#] tools, spent [x] input tokens and [y] output tokens") when the bot replies, and display lease timeout warnings if the turn expires. | Medium |
| **GAP-187** | **Console WebUI Coupling, Static Versioning, and 24h Spend Tile** | §6.5 (Console Headless Principle), §6.15: The web Console (`packages/console`) is coupled in deploy automation and versioning to the factory kernel: (1) all packages share static `0.1.0` in `package.json` and `server.ts` `/healthz` hardcodes `0.1.0`; (2) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` forces full multi-container `compose-proof` on UI-only changes; (3) FleetView KPI card displays `24h Egress Spend` (`currentSpendUsd`) rather than Total Monthly Cost (`currentSpendMonthlyUsd` and monthly budget ceiling). The Factory has 4 independently deployable units (Console, Control Plane, Gatekeeper Egress, Gatekeeper Ingress); Console requires independent SemVer versioning, dynamic health check version reporting, path-scoped CI, and monthly cost KPI presentation. | Medium |
| **GAP-188** | **Agent Warm-Down Idle Timeout Collides with Wall-Clock Cap and Sets Agent to ERROR** | §6.15 (Property Manager / Compute Lifecycle): In production, conversational agents entering a warm-down window (`warmDownSeconds: 3600`) transition into `ERROR` state after completing their runs. Three defects cause this: (1) In `scheduleTimeout`, `capMs` evaluates to `state.idleMs - (Date.now() - started)`. Because turn processing time is subtracted from `state.idleMs` (default 3600s), `capMs` is strictly less than 3600s, firing `TIMED_OUT` before the cartridge's 3600s warm-down window can finish naturally; (2) In `finishRun`, `agent.state = terminal === 'DONE' ? 'SLEEPING' : 'ERROR'`, incorrectly forcing `agent.state = 'ERROR'` when a run ends on idle timeout (`TIMED_OUT`) instead of `SLEEPING`; (3) When the agent receives the mailbox `done` signal, writes `/tmp/factory-result-<runId>.json: { "status": "succeeded" }` and posts `POST /api/v1/runs/<runId>/result`, `finishRun` bails out (`isTerminal`) and discards the clean result. | High |
| **GAP-189** | **Discord Turn Summary Always Reports 0 Input and 0 Output Tokens** | §6.5, GAP-186, TSK-252: the egress meters tokens and puts `inputTokens`/`outputTokens` on each model `call.end`, but the Inspector's `sanitizeProgress` (`packages/inspector/src/run-progress.ts`) rebuilds each event from a field whitelist that omits both, and the control plane relays that sanitized event to gatekeeper-ingress. The ingress therefore sums `undefined` and the in-channel summary reads "spent 0 input tokens and 0 output tokens" for every turn (seen 2026-10-21 on Donna, 4 tool calls). TSK-252 missed it because `run-progress.ts` was not in its lock and its tests read events before the control plane. Not a new rule: §6.5 already declares the fields. Known and left out: the summary is computed when the bot's reply lands, so the last model call's `call.end` can arrive after it (batching plus two hops) and be missed. | High |
| **GAP-190** | **No Command-Line Surface for the Factory (Mac CLI + Factory Manager)** | §6.17 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-21): the factory has the web console but no terminal client. Wanted: a macOS CLI, pointed at a factory instance, that opens a conversation with a Factory Manager (an optional house-staff cartridge) for fleet questions and actions by role (cost, list agents, budgets, and anything the web console does), and can connect the user to an agent to converse with it directly. Applies A1, A2, E12, SV1, L2, L5, AP1 to AP3 and the Donna Test; no new interface (KPF: API, webhooks, WebSockets, events). Open: (1) only admins can use the manager today: a non-admin Access sign-in is a `viewer`, and `agents.converse` needs `operator`, so widening it is a Bouncer and Access decision; (2) admitting the Factory Manager to a deployed factory is not scripted (`submind-aws` `agent-deploy` admits `SM-<id>` repositories only); (3) the Keychain, `cloudflared` login and a real model driving the tools are untested from the build environment. | High |
| **GAP-191** | **The CLI Cannot Open Without an Instance and a Sign-In** | §6.17 CL1, CL8 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-21): `factory` with no saved address or sign-in exits with an error and offers no way to set them but `factory login <url>`. Wanted: it opens to a splash screen, offers login, and `/config` (factory address, terminal theme) and `/login` (sign in, sign out, switch user) work at any time. | Medium |
| **GAP-192** | **The CLI Signs In Only Through Cloudflare Access** | §6.17 CL1, CL9 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-22): the factory already verifies OIDC tokens (`FACTORY_OIDC_ISSUER`, `FACTORY_OIDC_AUDIENCE`) but publishes nothing a CLI can sign in with (a public client id, scopes, which token), and the CLI knows only the Access browser flow. A deployment on Entra, Google, Cognito or Okta cannot use the CLI. Open: the landing zones do not yet pass the `FACTORY_OIDC_CLI_*` settings through; not tested against a real provider. | Medium |
| **GAP-193** | **No Single-Command Install of the CLI From an Ops Repository** | §6.17 CL10 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-22): installing the CLI is clone, build and link by hand, and each person types the production address. Wanted: one command from the ops repository that builds the CLI at the pinned factory commit and configures the deployment's address. `submind-aws` first; `submind-gcp` does not exist yet. | Medium |
| **GAP-194** | **The CLI Is Useless Without the Factory Manager** | §6.17 CL11 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-22): every interaction goes through a conversation with an optional cartridge that must be admitted, have a policy (the `models` route, a budget) and pay per question, and which a viewer cannot talk to (`agents.converse` needs `operator`). Reads the CLI already makes for the manager (agents, spend, budgets, runs, approvals) have no direct form, so a factory with no manager, an over-budget one, or a person who is only a viewer gets nothing, and nothing can be scripted. | Medium |
| **GAP-195** | **An Agent's Soul, Roles, Access and Memory Settings Live Only in Its Repository** | §6.18 CF1 to CF3, SK3, E12, AM1 (Dale, chat, 2026-10-23, design note "one runtime, agents defined by configuration"): the factory versions an agent's source, adopted skills, policy and owners, but its persona, roles (what each may use) and memory archetype are read from `cartridge.yaml` and `soul.md` in the agent's repository at admission. Changing any of them is a commit, a pull request on a repository, admission and a redeploy; the factory cannot show, version, ledger or export them; an owner cannot give a person a role on their own agent (only an admin can, TSK-173); an agent cannot ask for one; and an agent cannot move to another factory with its configuration. | High |
| **GAP-196** | **Approvals Can Be Decided Only in the Console or Through the API** | §6.18 CF4, E9, GAP-161: a held action or an elevated change waits for a person to open the console. The person is usually in a chat channel; nothing asks them there. A reply in a channel is routed to the agent, so approval cannot safely be carried by chat today (the agent could forge or relay it). `gatekeeper-ingress` has no way to post an interactive message or to intercept an approval reply. | High |
| **GAP-197** | **Every Agent Carries Its Own Copy of the Loop, Roles and Memory Code** | §6.18 RT1, RT2, AM1 to AM5, the Donna Test: `rbac.py` exists in five versions across ten agents and `memory/` in five versions across seven; `starter-python` has no `rbac.py`. The turn loop (`process_turn` and the tool dispatch) has the same shape in every agent sampled, so a fix to roles or memory reaches an agent only through that agent's own repository. Whether the loop is identical line for line is not verified. There is no shared runtime that runs an agent from its configuration. | High |
| **GAP-198** | **Every Skill Version Needs an Admin, Even a Private Skill for One Agent** | §6.18 CF5, SK1, SK3: a private skill belongs to one agent and can be adopted by no other, yet each of its versions waits for a factory admin, so an owner cannot get a skill of their own agent into it without one. Public skills must still be an admin's decision. Approving a skill version also needs to be stated as granting nothing (SK2): any route, credential or model it needs is a policy change, an elevated change that needs approval (CF4). | Medium |
| **GAP-199** | **New Agents Can Be Created Only From a Repository** | §6.18 CF6, L2, L3, SK8: registering an agent means naming a repository with a `cartridge.yaml`, so every new agent starts with a copy of the loop (GAP-197). Once agent configuration is the factory's (CF1) a new agent should be created from a configuration and run by the shared runtime (RT1), with no repository (an image is built only when it adopts skills, SK4). Existing agents keep running from their repositories until their owners decide otherwise. | Medium |

It fails on: a definition, in any other package's source, of a function, constant or class the Executive owns (`signV4`, `awsCredentialsFromEnv`, `SseMeter`, `usageFromJson`, `parseModelCatalog`, `parseChatRequest`, `defaultModelAdapters`, `bedrockConverse`, `toConverse`, `fromConverse`, `checkModel`, `offeredModels`, `findModel`, `ModelUpstreamError`) or of one of its types (`ChatRequest`, `ChatResult`, `ChatMessage`, `ModelAdapter`, `ModelCatalog`, `ModelPolicy`, `ModelCheck`, `AwsCredentials`, `BedrockConverseOptions`); in any other package's source, the literals only the Executive writes (the refusal and validation codes only the Executive answers (`model_not_allowed`, `model_pinned`, `messages_required`, `unsupported_role`, `unsupported_content`); `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256`; `bedrock-runtime`; Anthropic's `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens`); and a back-dependency in the Executive's manifest or imports. The patterns are proved on sample lines (forbidden and allowed) and against the real originals in `packages/executive`, and the guard was shown to fail on five planted violations in the real tree (a second `checkModel` in the egress, the refusal literal in the control plane, the signing scheme in an unrelated package, the Executive depending on the egress in its manifest and in its source), each caught by the right check, then restored. **Dropped on purpose, said in the file (never `it.skip`):** `complete`, `Usage`, `Provider`, `Completion` and `CatalogEntry` (ordinary names another member may use; the Keymaster has its own `CatalogEntry`); `FACTORY_MODEL_CATALOG` (the egress's `main.ts` is the composition root that reads the operations config and hands the string to `parseModelCatalog`; reading it is not a second copy); `'model_not_offered'` (the egress's own refusal when `findModel` finds nothing); `prompt_tokens` and `completion_tokens` (the egress builds the OpenAI-format response in `handleModels`, which stays the Gatekeeper's). **Hardened 2026-10-10 after the review of PR #134 (Claude).** The definition check reads every package's source in `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.js`, `.mjs` and `.cjs` (it had read only `control-plane`, `gatekeeper-egress` and `gatekeeper-ingress`, in `.ts`); it also catches an export or import renamed to an Executive name and an object method or property of that name; the guard's names and codes are confirmed in the real Executive source (see the second hardening below for which codes remain); and the back-dependency check reads whole files, so it catches `from`, a bare `import 'x'`, `import()` and `require()`, on one line or several. The review's other points on this row are covered above: `FACTORY_MODEL_CATALOG` was never in the shipped guard (this row's description now says what shipped). Checked on eight further planted violations in the real tree (an alias export, an object method, a definition in a package outside the old list, a definition in a `.tsx` file, and the Executive using `import()`, `require()`, a bare import and a multi-line `from`), each caught, then restored. **Hardened again 2026-10-10 after a second review of PR #134 (Claude).** (1) The rename check had also fired on an ordinary cast such as `JSON.parse(raw) as ChatRequest`, a normal use of the Executive's types that would have failed an unrelated PR; it now looks only inside `import { … }` and `export { … }` lists, on one line or several. (2) The generic codes `upstream_error`, `upstream_throttled` and `provider_unavailable` are no longer banned: another member (the Keymaster, the control plane) may legitimately answer with them, and the adapters are guarded by what only they write (`bedrock-runtime`, `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256`). The chat-request validation codes and the two model-policy codes remain. (3) Definitions are matched across line breaks (`export const` then the name on the next line; a method whose parameters wrap), and the file's header says what text matching still misses. (4) The back-dependency check also catches a relative path through `packages/` (`../../../packages/gatekeeper-egress/…`; a deep package specifier such as `…/dist/x.js` was already caught), reports every match instead of the first in a file, and ignores commented-out lines. (5) The check that the guard is not vacuous scans all of `packages/executive/src` for each name, so moving code to a new file inside the Executive is not a failure. (6) A new check asserts `packages/executive` is attributed to the Executive alone, which is what gives its files an owner (`owns` covers only the two shared packages). Checked on thirteen planted cases in the real tree: nine violations (an alias export, an object method, a wrapped-parameter method, a definition split across lines, a definition in a `.tsx` file, and the Executive using a `../../../packages/` path, a deep specifier, `import()` and a multi-line `from`) each failed the guard, and four legitimate cases (an ordinary cast, the generic codes in another package, a commented-out import, and renaming a file inside the Executive) each passed. All were restored. |

Rules carried from TSK-157 (review of #129): switch `approvedSkill` to the Registrar's `canAdopt` and refuse registration with `identityIssue`; refuse `already_adopted` before writing, because a configuration `put` that changes nothing creates no version and drops the new provenance; only an explicit request by an owner or an admin calls `AdoptionStore.request` for a revoked private skill (it lifts the block on automatic adoption), the automatic path asks `blocksAutoAdopt` and never calls it. | `COMPLETED` | *None* (Released) | `packages/control-plane/src/skills.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/skills*.test.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/skills.adoption.e2e.test.ts` (new), `packages/control-plane/package.json` (test script lists the new file), `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts` (one dispatch line), `packages/control-plane/src/config-store.ts` (export `ledgerVersion`), `packages/bouncer/src/privileges.ts`, `packages/bouncer/src/*.test.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/route-privileges.e2e.test.ts` (the privilege matrix), `docs/SKILLS.md` | Adoption is a configuration change with its own approval, ledgered and role-gated; the adopters API answers who uses each skill. **Resolved 2026-10-10 (Claude).** Routes as listed, with the adopters list adding `upgradeAvailable`; `GET /skills` and `/skills/:id` also show `adopters`. Four Bouncer privileges (`skills.adopt.request`, `skills.adopt.remove` for an admin and the agent's owner, agent-scoped; `skills.adopt.decide`, `skills.retire` for an admin only), added to the census, the matrix test and the owner-rights test, which proves an owner never decides. Approving a version of a private skill adopts it for its owner (a failure is ledgered `SKILL_ADOPTION_FAILED` and leaves a request, and never undoes the approval); a revocation blocks that. Registration refuses a version that would change visibility or owner, a private skill whose owner agent is unknown or whose registrant is neither an admin nor that agent's owner, and any version of a retired skill; approval refuses a retired version. Each agent's adoption changes run one at a time (tested: the concurrency test fails without the lock). Every change is a configuration version with provenance and a ledger row (`SKILL_ADOPTION_REQUESTED`, `_APPROVED`, `_REJECTED`, `_REMOVED`, `SKILL_ADOPTED_PRIVATE`, `SKILL_ADOPTION_FAILED`, `SKILL_RETIRED`, `SKILL_RETIRED_FORCED`). Private skills are hidden from viewers in the catalog, the adopters list and the agent's skills list; the configuration API still shows them (GAP-125). Not in this slice: the build and redeploy that make an adoption run (TSK-159; a forced retire leaves the agents paused, with the skill removed from their configuration, until then), the E8 ceiling check on a request (the approver is shown what access it adds; the policy still decides), and per-action `hold` enforcement (GAP-070). **Review of #131 (2026-10-10):** a rejection now runs under the agent's lock, so it cannot land while the same request is being approved; a forced retire marks the skill as being retired (no adoption may start), waits for writes already under way, and repeats until no agent adopts it, so none is left pinned to a retired skill; the response adds `pauseFailed`; a new request reports the pending one it replaced; a malformed body is refused rather than read as empty. Each is pinned by a test that fails when the mechanism is removed. A request is also made under the agent's lock, so an approval being written cannot clear a newer request for the same skill. Recorded, not changed: a forced retire does not stop when an agent cannot be paused (an unknown or built-in agent, which the kill switch refuses); the locks are per process, as the control plane's stores are; the retire waits for every agent's lock, which is broader than needed but does not depend on an invariant a later change could break. |

Control plane: a registration whose roles or routes are badly declared is refused (422 `invalid_roles`, the reasons, a ledger row `AGENT_REGISTRATION_REFUSED:roles`, nothing recorded); re-registering replaces them; `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/roles` returns the roles, the `assignable` names (never `Owner`) and each skill's routes, under a new privilege `agents.roles.read` (admin and the agent's owner, agent-scoped; not a viewer's, because a role can name a private skill). Checked against all 12 real cartridges: none names an undeclared skill, so no existing agent is refused. Tests at each layer; seven mutations of the new rules are each caught, plus five in the contract. **Noticed, not changed:** the roles are now part of the agent record, which any viewer can read through `GET /api/v1/registry/agents`, as they can its egress and credential declarations; role names and skill ids are not secrets, but a role can name a private skill's id. | **TSK-177** | GAP-131 | **[Scheduled-run requester] A scheduled run carries the authority of whoever requested it, never more (GitHub issue #139 section 3).** (1) A schedule records `createdVia` (`agent`, `console` or `api`) and `requestedBy` (an external identity, a Cloudflare principal, or `system`). The factory derives `requestedBy` itself: from the verified run's recorded caller when an agent creates it, from the authenticated principal otherwise; a request body or an agent can never name it, and a run that is itself scheduled passes on its own requester so authority cannot grow through a chain. A schedule saved before this change has no requester and is read as `system` (Dale, chat, 2026-10-10: all existing schedules are his). (2) When a schedule fires, the factory re-resolves the requester's current roles by the rule a person's message uses (`agents.wake` on that agent) and builds a verified `caller` badge for `createRun` (the Bouncer's shape, plus `source: 'schedule'` and the schedule name); a requester who no longer holds a role has the fire skipped and ledgered (`SCHEDULE_SKIPPED_UNAUTHORIZED`). A `system` requester gets the badge `{actor: 'factory:scheduler', role: 'system'}`, which is the agent's own authority. (3) The run records the requester, so the egress (TSK-174) sees who it is for. Not in this task: `PATCH`/`paused`, the scheduling skill and any console view (issue #139 steps 2 and 3); the cartridge side is `SM-donna` pull request 21, outside this repository. | `DONE` | Claude (Sonnet 5.5) - 2026-10-10 | `DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md`, `packages/timekeeper/src/schedules.ts`, `packages/timekeeper/src/index.ts`, `packages/timekeeper/src/*.test.ts`, `packages/timekeeper/package.json`, `packages/control-plane/src/schedules.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/schedules.e2e.test.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/schedules.requester.e2e.test.ts` (new), `packages/control-plane/src/index.ts`, `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts`, `packages/control-plane/package.json`, `packages/bouncer/src/identity.ts`, `packages/bouncer/src/*.test.ts`, `packages/bouncer/package.json`, `docs/CARTRIDGE_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md` | A schedule Dale made runs as Dale and Donna's morning debrief is no longer refused; a schedule a Family member makes through Donna can use only what Family may; removing the role stops it. **Resolved:** schedules record `createdVia` and `requestedBy` (types in the Timekeeper, derived in `control-plane/src/schedules.ts`). `requesterOfRun` reads a run's requester from the factory's own record (a schedule's own for a scheduled run, the system for a cartridge cron, the identity or principal otherwise) and creation is refused when it cannot tell. `scheduleRunOptions` re-resolves the requester by the `agents.wake` rule at every fire, builds the badge (`source: 'schedule'`, `scheduleName`) and records the identity on the run, or skips and ledgers; a malformed requester is never read as the system. `AuthenticatedCaller.role` gains `system`. **Noticed, not changed:** (1) `createdVia` is `agent` or `api`; `console` needs the console's proxy to say so (a console task). (2) A cartridge's own declared cron still starts with no badge and no input; making that the system badge changes every cartridge's cron payload and is a separate decision. (3) Whether a role may create a schedule at all is the scheduling skill's permission (issue #139 step 3), not checked here. (4) The cartridge side is SM-donna pull request 21: until it merges, Donna describes a system badge as an unauthorised scheduled task. |
