### Treasurer flow work stream (TSK-080, TSK-084 to TSK-090)

**Intent.** The Treasurer owns the budget rule, spend accounting and pricing in `packages/budget`. The flow: the Treasurer records the budget (set by an admin through the policy API); the Executive reports each model call's usage and the Treasurer prices it and records it with the Auditor; before each Executive access the Gatekeeper asks the Treasurer whether the agent is in good standing, and before each wake the Landlord does the same. The Gatekeeper and Landlord enforce; the Treasurer decides.

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-03):** the flow above, and the split into the small tasks on this board.
**Defaults chosen by the drafter, to be confirmed by Dale before TSK-088 is locked:** the refusal at wake is HTTP 402 `budget_exceeded` (matching the gatekeeper-egress); the `BLOCKED_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` run state stays as it is; `gatekeeper-ingress` relays the Landlord's refusal instead of calling a separate standing endpoint; a queued (SQS) message refused with 402 stays in the queue and runs once the budget is raised, and the queue trigger writes no refusal ledger row so redelivery does not flood the ledger; the egress keeps writing the `llm` ledger row itself (making the Executive-to-Treasurer-to-Auditor hand-off separate runtime hops is out of scope for this stream). TSK-080 to TSK-087 do not depend on these.

**How to work it.** Tasks are tagged `[Treasurer flow n/8]`. Take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING` and whose `after` tasks are `COMPLETED`; do that one task, commit, and stop unless told to continue. Follow §2 for each (cite the invariants, take the lock listing every file, edit only inside it, close and unlock). Branch `feat/treasurer-flow` from an up-to-date `main` (PR #76 is merged, so `feat/factory-service-taxonomy` is stale); one commit per task, subject `refactor(budget): <what> (TSK-0xx)`; never push to `main`; do not push or open a PR unless asked.

**Every task must be green on its own.** Run, in order: `npm install` (once per clone), `npm test` (builds everything, then all workspace tests), `npm run validate`, `./scripts/conformance.sh`, and the image builds or `./scripts/compose-e2e.sh` for any task that adds a dependency edge or changes how a run starts, is refused or ends (`compose-proof` runs that script in CI and is a required check). Do not edit assertions in `spend.e2e.test.ts`, `routes.test.ts`, `gatekeeper-egress.test.ts` or `models.test.ts` to make a move pass; import lines are fine. No `.skip`, no `--no-verify`, no weakening a conformance check.

**Traps.**
- Do not recreate the removed attempt (`git show 6437f15:packages/budget/src/index.ts`): it was a new engine with a hardcoded price table, never wired. This stream moves existing code.
- A new dependency edge is not finished until all five places are updated: the package's `package.json`, the root `package.json` build list, `scripts/conformance.sh`, both Dockerfiles (builder `-w`, runtime `COPY package.json`, runtime `COPY --from=builder ... dist`) and `package-lock.json`. Unit tests pass without the Dockerfile edits while the images and `compose-proof` break.
- `packages/budget/dist/` is stale, gitignored output from the removed attempt; delete it before building.
- Package `test` scripts list files explicitly; a new `*.test.ts` that is not added to the list never runs.
- The Donna Test does not apply: this is Factory code (spend, perimeter, wake), not an agent's behavior. Do not add interfaces beyond API, webhooks, WebSockets and events (`KPF.md`).

### Bouncer flow work stream (TSK-094 to TSK-109)

**Intent.** The Bouncer decides who may do what and holds what must wait for a person. `packages/bouncer` holds the approval record, the E9 hold rules and the privilege contract. The Gatekeeper authenticates (it verifies a credential and says who the caller is and which roles that person holds); the Bouncer authorizes (it maps roles to privileges and answers whether this caller may do this). Every control-plane route asks the Bouncer for one named privilege, so a route moved by a later extraction arrives already authorized and a conformance check protects every move. For an agent's action the agent is the actor and the requesting user is the reviewer and approver; an agent has owners, and the `agent-owner` role gives them a defined set of rights on their own agents. Cited: SV1, SV2, E4, E7, E9, E10 (§6.3.1, §6.15), A2 (§6.12), K5 item 6 (§6.11), SK3 (§6.14), GAP-037. The Donna Test does not apply: this is who may act and what waits for sign-off, not an agent's reasoning or memory.

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-03):** authentication stays with the Gatekeeper and authorization belongs to the Bouncer; there is an `agent-owner` role and every agent can identify its owner or owners; access is role-based in all cases (users map to roles and roles map to privileges, never a user to a privilege); the actor and the approver are separate parties, and for an agent's action the agent is the actor and the requesting user is the reviewer and approver (the roadmap decisions); and, answering the Bouncer plan's questions: the role-to-privilege table is built into the code now, and making it factory data that an admin edits is a later enhancement (not in this stream); an agent's owners are stored on its configuration record (SK3); putting the control plane behind the Gatekeeper is out of this stream (`authorize` takes a principal the Gatekeeper's authentication has already verified, so it works either way); the Gatekeeper's ingress passes the Discord author id with the wake and an admin links Discord ids to people; when a run has no requesting user, any owner of the agent decides; the `agent-owner` role holds these rights on its own agents: decide held actions, view and export the agent's configuration, supply the agent's credentials, and wake, pause and resume the agent; and the global `admin` and `approver` roles keep deciding any agent's held action or tool approval (the requester and the owners are added on top, nobody loses access).

**Defaults chosen by the drafter, to be confirmed by Dale before the named task is locked** (they work out details of the decisions above; none is in a recorded decision):
- TSK-103: an owner is a principal actor string (`cloudflare:alice@example.com`, `oidc:...`, `token:...`), lower-cased, at most 10 per agent; `PUT /api/v1/agents/:id/owners` is admin only; an owner change that cannot be stored fails instead of looking stored.
- TSK-105: "supply the agent's credentials" means the credentials routes for that one agent plus starting and importing that agent's connections; the fleet-wide outstanding list, platform credentials and the provider's own client secret stay admin. `isolate`, cancelling a run and the conversation hand-off stay operator. MCP tools stay role-only. Exporting one agent's configuration is `GET /api/v1/config/export?agent=<id>`.
- TSK-106: the requester belongs to the run: the person whose message started it; a later message in a live run does not change it. Only a caller with the `gatekeeper-ingress` role may attest a requester (TSK-111, GAP-092; before that any `operator` or `admin` could, which let a linked operator name their own id and approve their own request). Cron, webhook, queue, MCP and approval-delivery runs record none.
- TSK-107: the link store was first said to belong to the Gatekeeper as authentication data; Dale decided on 2026-10-12 (GAP-136) that it is the Bouncer's, a list of which Discord, Slack, Cloudflare or Entra identities are one person; route and ledger action names as written in the task.
- TSK-108: a requester whose Discord id is not linked lets no owner decide, so only the global `admin` and `approver` can; an owner decides only when the run has no requesting user.

**Open for Dale** (none blocks TSK-094 to TSK-102, the moves, and each has a default that keeps today's behavior):
- Separate proposer and approver: a system definition (E10) and a skill version (SK1) are proposed by any authenticated user and approved by an admin, who may be the same person. Should the Bouncer refuse an approver who is the proposer? Default: not enforced, no task.
- Admin-only actions have one party today (register, deploy, retire, reinstate, purge, set a budget or a policy, supply platform credentials). Should any need a second party? Default: no.
- Agent (run-token) access (`authenticateRun`, `/api/v1/spend` with the `factory-spend` policy tool, the schedule scoping to its own agent) is an agent identity check and stays as it is. Should an agent's own access become Bouncer privileges later? Default: no change here.
- A narrower role for the ingress service so it need not be an `operator` (a token and landing-zone change; production belongs in the ops repository, not here).
- Owner rights over MCP tools and removing the console's separate `hasRole` copy by serving privileges from `/api/v1/whoami` (new behavior in the API and the console).

**Parties to each privileged action** (the plan states them, per the decision; "today" is what the code does):

| Action type | Actor | Reviewer and approver |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| A held request in a person's name (E9) and a tool call marked `requireApproval` (E4) | The agent | The requesting user; when the run has none, any owner of the agent. The global `admin` and `approver` also may (confirmed). |
| Wake, pause, resume an agent | An operator, an owner of that agent, or a trigger (schedule, webhook, queue, the ingress) | None: reversible and ledgered. |
| Isolate, cancel a run, hand a conversation to a run | An operator or an admin | None. |
| Propose a system definition (E10) or register a skill (SK1) | Any authenticated user | An admin (may be the same person today; see Open). |
| Register, deploy, retire, reinstate, purge an agent; set a budget, a policy or the global budget; supply platform or provider credentials; set owners; link identities | An admin | None today (see Open). |
| Supply an agent's credentials, start or import its connection (K5, K2) | An admin or an owner of that agent | None: write-only and ledgered. |
| Ledger ingest, run lookups, holds, consumption, progress, connection tokens | The gatekeeper-egress service role (`ledger.ingest` also the `ingest` role) | None. |

**How to work it.** Tasks are tagged `[Bouncer flow n/16]`. Take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING` and whose `after` tasks are `COMPLETED`; do that one task, commit, and stop unless told to continue. Follow §2 for each (cite the invariants, take the lock listing every file, edit only inside it, close and unlock). Branch `feat/bouncer-flow` from an up-to-date `main`; one commit per task, subject `refactor(bouncer): <what> (TSK-0xx)` for TSK-094 to TSK-102, `feat(bouncer): <what> (TSK-0xx)` for TSK-103 to TSK-108 and `docs(bouncer): <what> (TSK-109)`; never push to `main`; open the pull request when Dale asks, preferably two: the moves (TSK-094 to TSK-102), then the new behavior (TSK-103 to TSK-109). Lanes: TSK-095, 096 and 097 edit the same gatekeeper-egress file; TSK-095, 096, 099, 101, 105, 106, 107 and 108 edit `app.ts`; TSK-094 to 098 and 103 to 108 edit the same bouncer files. None of those run in parallel with each other. Only TSK-100 (no `app.ts` line) may overlap with work in another package, after TSK-099. Pull requests touching `DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md`, `packages/conformance/` or `scripts/conformance.sh` (CODEOWNERS, TSK-073) go to Dale for review.

**Every task must be green on its own.** Run, in order: `npm install` (once per clone), `npm test` (builds everything, then all workspace tests), `npm run validate`, `./scripts/conformance.sh`, and for any task that adds a dependency edge (TSK-094, 095, 096, 098) both image builds or `./scripts/compose-e2e.sh`. TSK-105 and TSK-106 change who may start a run and what a run records: run `./scripts/compose-e2e.sh` for them too. It is not in their locks because it exercises no approval, owner or requester and the tokens it uses (`dev-admin-token`, and the ingress token with the `operator` role) keep every privilege they hold today, which the TSK-099 matrix proves. Do not edit assertions in `held.test.ts`, `held.e2e.test.ts`, `index.test.ts`, `access.e2e.test.ts`, `keymaster.test.ts`, `keymaster.e2e.test.ts`, `routes.test.ts` or `gatekeeper-egress.test.ts` to make a move pass; import lines are fine. From TSK-099 on, the matrix test stays green in every task and a task that adds a route adds its row. No `.skip`, no `--no-verify`, no weakening a conformance check.

**Privilege census** (what TSK-098 writes out; "today" is the role the route names). A request that passes is one that is neither 401 nor 403.

| `policy.read` | viewer | `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/policy` |

| `spend.budget.set` | admin | `PUT /api/v1/spend/budgets` |

That is 59 privileges and 61 `authenticate` call sites (a privilege can be asked at more than one site). Routes with no privilege, which the matrix lists as such: `/ui`, `/healthz`, `/`, `/api/v1/health` (public); `GET /api/v1/connections/:provider/callback` (public: the provider's redirect, authenticated by its signed state); `GET /api/v1/whoami` (any verified principal: `identify` only); `POST /api/v1/hooks/:id` with a `secretRef` (the cartridge's shared secret); and the run-token routes `GET` or `POST /api/v1/runs/:id/input`, `result`, `heartbeat` and `mailbox`, `GET /api/v1/runs/:id/events` and `POST /api/v1/keymaster/checkout`. Later tasks add `agents.owners.set` (TSK-103), `config.export.agent` (TSK-104), `runs.attest-requester` (TSK-106), `identity.links.read` and `identity.links.set` (TSK-107), `agents.reset` (TSK-114), and `spend.budget.set` (TSK-134): 66 in all.

**Traps.**
- A new dependency edge is not finished until all five places are updated: the package's `package.json`, the root `package.json` build list, `scripts/conformance.sh`, both Dockerfiles (builder `-w`, runtime `COPY package.json`, runtime `COPY --from=builder ... dist`) and `package-lock.json`. The edges here: `control-plane` to `bouncer` (TSK-094), `keymaster` and `gatekeeper-egress` to `bouncer` (TSK-095), `bouncer` to `ledger` (TSK-096) and `bouncer` to `auth` (TSK-098). Build order: bouncer after `ledger` and `budget` (and after `auth`), before `keymaster` and `gatekeeper-egress`, in the root list, `scripts/conformance.sh` and both Dockerfiles; the keymaster is only in the control-plane image and not in `scripts/conformance.sh`. Unit tests pass without the Dockerfile edits while the images and `compose-proof` break.
- SV1 fails for a workspace package that is in no service's `hostedIn`, and for a `hostedIn` path with no source: `packages/bouncer` and its first source file arrive in the same task (TSK-094). If TSK-092 has landed, the new package gets its root-only `exports` map; if TSK-093 has landed, each task that adds a non-test source file to `control-plane/src` or `gatekeeper-egress/src` (only TSK-107) adds it to `owns`. Whichever of TSK-092, TSK-093 and this stream lands second reconciles the other.
- Package `test` scripts list files explicitly and `hygiene.test.ts` fails CI on a test file no script names: bouncer's list grows in TSK-094, 095, 096, 097, 098 and 104 (`approvals`, `wire`, `hold`, `decisions`, `privileges`, `roles`), the control-plane list in TSK-099, 103, 105, 106, 107 and 108.
- The E9 hash must not change (TSK-096, pinned vectors) and the configuration hash of every existing version must not change (TSK-103, pinned vector; owners are `undefined`, never `[]`, when none are set).
- 401 and 403 bodies, and the MCP error text, stay byte for byte as they are until Dale decides otherwise; `required` names a role.
- `agent-owner`, `agent-member` and `requester` are derived from data (owners, the person's role on the agent from their identity link, the run's requester) and are never read from a token or an identity-provider claim. `ROLES` in `auth` does not list them and must not.
- Owners are authorization data held in a store whose write-through helper swallows failures (`recordConfig`). The owner write raises, and a missing store means no owners, never "everyone".
- `configOf` rebuilds a configuration from live state on every registration, policy change and deploy; it must carry `owners` forward or every re-registration silently removes them. If SK4 later tags images by the configuration hash, owners must not change the image tag.
- `run.input.authorId` is what the caller wrote; only `run.requestedBy`, set under `runs.attest-requester`, is an identity.
- `server.ts` in the ingress calls `listen` at import and cannot be imported by a test; the request body logic moves to `wake.ts` (TSK-106).
- Static bearer tokens must carry at least one valid role (`FACTORY_TOKENS` rejects an empty list), so a test principal with no role needs an Access service principal or an OIDC test key (see `access.e2e.test.ts`).
- The console's `hasRole` copy and the agent run-token rules are not changed here, and no second copy of the role table may appear anywhere (TSK-102 checks the control plane, gatekeeper-egress and ingress source).
- No new interfaces beyond API, webhooks, WebSockets and events (`KPF.md`): the new routes are API routes. Nothing here changes a landing zone or an ops repository (CLAUDE.md §1): the compose `FACTORY_TOKENS` and the AWS and GCP token secrets are unchanged and keep working.











### Executive flow work stream (TSK-147 to TSK-155)

**Status (2026-10-10):** TSK-147 to TSK-155 are `COMPLETED`; the extraction is done. What stays outside is GAP-130. The model configuration that follows (the owner chooses the model, a backup and a model per skill) is GAP-126 and TSK-165 to TSK-170.

**Intent.** The Executive is the Factory's model service. `packages/executive` holds the model catalog, the OpenAI-compatible request rules (M1), the provider adapters and request signing (Bedrock today), token counting (E5), and three typed contracts: `checkModel` (whether an agent may use a model: the allow-list, the factory default when a policy names none, the TRAINING exemption and a run's pin, M2, E7), `offeredModels` (the models an agent may list) and `complete` (serve one chat completion through the catalog's adapter). The Executive decides; the Gatekeeper's egress enforces the answer (`deny`, the 403 and 400 bodies), the Treasurer prices and decides standing, the Auditor records the `llm` row, the Inspector reports metrics and traces. The dependency points one way: the egress depends on the Executive; the Executive depends on the Treasurer for the `Price` and `TokenUsage` types only and never on the egress, the control plane or the console.

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-09):** record the plan and take the lock. The plan was shown as eight tasks, the three-function contract above (as opposed to a single `serve()` that would also move the handler), and the extraction as its own milestone before any model-selection behavior. Open decisions 1 to 6 in `docs/executive-roadmap.md` stay open; the extraction depends on none of them, and `complete`'s signature is kept narrow because decisions 3 and 4 may add a field.

**Defaults (explained to Dale in plain words; go-ahead given, chat, 2026-10-10: "yes to all three, go ahead"):** `checkModel` takes the policy's allow-list, the default model name, the pin and the agent's training state as plain inputs (it does not import the control plane's `Policy`); provider-native routes (`handleLlm`) call `checkModel` too and otherwise stay as they are (M4).

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-10):** model choice is configuration, not code (M2 above, GAP-126). The extraction (TSK-148 to TSK-155) is unchanged and is moves only; the new behavior is the model configuration work stream (TSK-165 to TSK-170), which comes after it.

**Pull requests:** #125 (the plan, TSK-147: no code); #128 (TSK-148 to TSK-150, the moves); #132 (TSK-151 and TSK-152, the contracts, with the model configuration rule); #134 (TSK-153 to TSK-155: the file map, the guard, this entry).

**Traps.**
- A new dependency edge is not finished until all five places are updated (as in the Registrar stream): the package's `package.json`, the root `package.json` build list, `scripts/conformance.sh`, the Dockerfile (builder `-w`, runtime `COPY package.json`, runtime `COPY --from=builder ... dist`) and `package-lock.json`. The image builds only the packages it lists; `npm test` will not catch a missing line.
- Package `test` scripts list files explicitly: a moved `*.test.ts` must be removed from the egress list and added to the Executive's, or it never runs.
- A file moved out of `gatekeeper-egress` must come off the Executive's `owns` in the same task, or the TSK-093 file-map check fails on a claimed file that does not exist. The SV1 check requires an owned file in every `hostedIn` package, so `hostedIn` changes in the task that moves the first file.
- The egress's own instruments (`factory.gatekeeper-egress.*`, GAP-109) and the `llm` ledger write in `recordUsage` stay in the egress. Do not move them.
- `gatekeeper-egress.test.ts` tests the model route through `createGatekeeperEgress` with a fake adapter; it must pass unedited apart from imports through TSK-152. A changed response body or status is a behavior change and out of scope.
- Do not add an interface beyond API, webhooks, WebSockets and events (`KPF.md`). The Donna Test does not apply: this is Factory code. Prices and the catalog stay operations data (M3).
- Two lanes: none of these tasks edits `control-plane/src/app.ts`, so they may run in parallel with `app.ts` work, but TSK-148 to TSK-153 edit `gatekeeper-egress.ts` or its `index.ts` and run in series with each other and with any other task that edits those files (TSK-146 does).

**Verify (each task):** `npm test`, `npm run validate`, `./scripts/conformance.sh`; build the egress image where a Docker daemon exists (CI's `compose-proof` otherwise).

### Timekeeper flow work stream (TSK-117 to TSK-123)

**Intent.** The Timekeeper decides when an agent is due: it owns the cron rule, time zones and cron validation, the record and store of dynamic schedules, and the clock that notices a schedule or a cartridge cron is due. `packages/timekeeper` holds all of that behind one typed contract, `createTimekeeper`, and it never starts a run: it says "this agent is due" through a `fire` callback, and the composition root gives that callback to the Landlord (`createRun`). The dependency points one way: the control plane depends on the Timekeeper, never the reverse. The `/api/v1/schedules` handler stays in `control-plane`, because it is where the Gatekeeper's run-token rule (GAP-089), the Bouncer's privileges and the Auditor's ledger rows meet a request.

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-06):** (1) this extraction is moves only; one-shot wakeup timers are not part of it (GAP-096 records that the role text names a capability that does not exist; build it or remove the words later); (2) the schedule HTTP handlers stay in `control-plane`, as the Bouncer's routes did, and call the package.

**Defaults chosen by the drafter, confirmed by Dale as written (chat, 2026-10-06, when TSK-117 was handed to an executor):**
- TSK-117: the package is `@beercanlabs/factory-timekeeper`; the five dependency-edge places and the Timekeeper's `hostedIn` change land in the same task (SV1 fails on an unattributed package).
- TSK-119 and TSK-120: the contract is a structural agent type and a `fire` callback, so the package imports neither the Registrar's `AgentRecord` nor the Landlord's `createRun`.
- TSK-121: skip-if-already-running for a cartridge cron stays in the composition root's `fire`; a dynamic schedule fires whether or not the agent is running, as today.
- The schedule file stays where and as it is (`FACTORY_SCHEDULES_PATH`, else `DATA_DIR/schedules.json`); no format change.

**Who checked this plan.** The executor checked each task against the code before editing it (TSK-117 onward) and Dale reviewed and merged each pull request. All seven tasks are `COMPLETED` (2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07).

**How to work it.** Tasks are tagged `[Timekeeper flow n/7]`. Take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING` and whose predecessors are `COMPLETED`; do that one task, commit, and stop unless told to continue. Follow §2 for each (cite the invariants, take the lock listing every file, edit only inside it, close and unlock). Branch `feat/timekeeper-flow` from an up-to-date `main`; one commit per task, subject `refactor(timekeeper): <what> (TSK-1xx)`; never push to `main`; do not push or open a PR unless asked. TSK-118 edits `app.ts`, so it runs in series with every other task that edits it.

**Every task must be green on its own.** Run, in order: `npm install` (once per clone), `npm test` (builds everything, then all workspace tests), `npm run validate`, `./scripts/conformance.sh`, and the image builds or `./scripts/compose-e2e.sh` for any task that adds a dependency edge or changes how a run starts (TSK-117 and TSK-121). Do not edit assertions in `schedules.e2e.test.ts`, `wake-budget.e2e.test.ts` or `access.e2e.test.ts` to make a move pass; import lines are fine. No `.skip`, no `--no-verify`, no weakening a conformance check.

**Traps.**
- A new dependency edge is not finished until all five places are updated: the package's `package.json`, the root `package.json` build list, `scripts/conformance.sh`, the Dockerfile (builder `-w`, runtime `COPY package.json`, runtime `COPY --from=builder ... dist`) and `package-lock.json`. Unit tests pass without the Dockerfile edit while the image and `compose-proof` break. Only the control-plane image needs the Timekeeper.
- A file moved out of `control-plane` must come off the Timekeeper's `owns` in `FACTORY_SERVICES` in the same task, or the TSK-093 file-map check fails on a claimed file that does not exist; and a `hostedIn` entry in a shared package needs an owned file there, or the same check fails.
- Package `test` scripts list files explicitly; a new `*.test.ts` that is not added to the list never runs.
- `scheduleTimeout` in `app.ts` is the run timeout (the Landlord's), not the Timekeeper's. Do not move it.
- `index.ts` listens at import, so the loop cannot be unit-tested there; that is why the loop's logic moves into `tick` first (TSK-120) and TSK-121 only wires it.
- The Donna Test does not apply: this is Factory code (when an agent is due and who asks for the run), not an agent's behavior. Do not add interfaces beyond API, webhooks, WebSockets and events (`KPF.md`).

### Registrar flow work stream (TSK-124 to TSK-132)

**Status (2026-10-08):** TSK-124 to TSK-132 are `COMPLETED`; the extraction is done. What stays outside is GAP-099 to GAP-101; the agent version flow is GAP-037 and `docs/registrar-roadmap.md`.

**Intent.** The Registrar decides what may be admitted and what is recorded about it: the agent record and its registry store, the pinned source (repo and commit, L3), the skill registry and its checks (SK1 to SK5), the versioned deployment configuration (SK3), the systems store (E10), and the admission decision that only a pinned, admitted commit deploys (L4). `packages/registrar` holds all of that behind typed contracts, and it never builds an image, starts a run or answers an HTTP request: the Landlord builds and runs, and the control plane's handlers meet the request. The dependency points one way: the control plane, the Landlord (`runtime*.ts`, `aws/codebuild.ts`) and the Keymaster (`credentials.ts`) depend on the Registrar, never the reverse. Where the Registrar needs something that is another member's (a privilege check, a ledger row, the kill switch, an image build) it takes a callback or a structural type, as the Timekeeper's `fire` does.

**Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-07):** the work ships as **five pull requests**, each of one or two tasks with one commit per task (the tasks below are tagged with their PR); this extraction is moves only (see "Defaults").

**Defaults chosen by the drafter, confirmed by Dale as written (chat, 2026-10-07: "Yes on defaults").** D2 is confirmed as "the systems store stays last and its owner is decided when TSK-128 comes up", not as a decision about where it lives.
- D1: the HTTP handlers (`handleConfig`, `handleSkills`, `handleSystems` and the registry and deploy routes) stay in `control-plane`, because that is where the Bouncer's privileges, the Auditor's ledger rows and the Gatekeeper's run-token rule meet a request (the Timekeeper's and the Bouncer's precedent). They call the package.
- D2: `systems.ts` stays last (TSK-128). Ownership was never in question: the §6.15 map gives the systems store to the Registrar, and agents, skills and systems are what is registered with it. The open point was only wiring: two methods of the store call the Keymaster's `connectionProviderFromSystem`. **Confirmed by Dale (chat, 2026-10-08: "Option A, go ahead with TSK-128"):** the store moves to the Registrar without those two methods and without a Keymaster dependency; the control plane keeps a thin subclass of it that adds them. (This entry first listed the Keymaster or the Gatekeeper owning the store as an option; that was a mistake in the wording and is withdrawn.)
- D3: `config-store.ts` meets `policy.ts` (`archiveStamp`, `isReservedPolicyId`, `AgentPolicy`) and `identity-links.ts` (`parseActor`). The package takes these as injected functions and a structural policy type; it does not import `policy.ts` or `identity-links.ts`, which are the Gatekeeper's (GAP-089).
- D4: `AgentRecord` lives in `packages/registrar`. The Landlord and the Keymaster import it from there.
- D5: the admission contract (TSK-129) is a move: the commit-pinning and refusal logic the deploy route has today, behind `admit`, with the image build injected. New admission behavior is its own later task.
- The registry file layout (`registryDir/<id>.json`) and the configuration store's on-disk format do not change.
- The package is `@beercanlabs/factory-registrar`; `FULL_SHA` moves with the source pinning (it is L3's rule, and it is defined in the Landlord's `runtime.ts` today only because that was the first file to need it).

**Who checked this plan.** Drafted from reading `catalog.ts`, `source.ts` and `skill-checks.ts` in full and the import graph of the other files; `config-store.ts`, `skills.ts`, `systems.ts` and the deploy route were read for their imports and entry points, not line by line. The executor of each task checks it against the code before editing it, and says so in the task notes.

**How to work it.** Tasks are tagged `[Registrar flow n/9]` and grouped into five pull requests: **PR 1** TSK-124; **PR 2** TSK-125 and TSK-126; **PR 3** TSK-127 alone (Dale, chat, 2026-10-08: "treat that as my review and merge it"; TSK-128 waits on D2 and ships as its own pull request, so the work stream is six pull requests); **PR 4** TSK-129 and TSK-130; **PR 5** TSK-131 and TSK-132. Take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING` and whose predecessors are `COMPLETED`; do the tasks of one PR, one commit per task, subject `refactor(registrar): <what> (TSK-1xx)`, and stop at the end of the PR unless told to continue. Follow §2 for each (cite the invariants, take the lock listing every file, edit only inside it, close and unlock). One branch per PR, from an up-to-date `main` after the previous PR has merged; never push to `main`. TSK-125, TSK-129 and TSK-130 edit `app.ts` (TSK-124 touches only its import lines, and TSK-126 to TSK-128 only its dispatch and import lines), so each of those runs in series with every other task that edits `app.ts`.

**Every task must be green on its own.** Run, in order: `npm install` (once per clone), `npm test` (builds everything, then all workspace tests), `npm run validate`, `./scripts/conformance.sh`, and the image build or `./scripts/compose-e2e.sh` for any task that adds a dependency edge or changes how a deploy runs (TSK-124 and TSK-129). Do not edit assertions in `registry.e2e.test.ts`, `admission.e2e.test.ts`, `skills.e2e.test.ts`, `config-store.e2e.test.ts` or `systems.e2e.test.ts` to make a move pass; import lines are fine. No `.skip`, no `--no-verify`, no weakening a conformance check.

**Traps.**
- A new dependency edge is not finished until all five places are updated: the package's `package.json`, the root `package.json` build list, `scripts/conformance.sh`, the Dockerfile (builder `-w`, runtime `COPY package.json`, runtime `COPY --from=builder ... dist`) and `package-lock.json`. Unit tests pass without the Dockerfile edit while the image and `compose-proof` break. Only the control-plane image needs the Registrar.
- `package-lock.json`: `npm install` on some machines also strips `libc` fields from unrelated entries. Keep only the hunks for the new package.
- A file moved out of `control-plane` must come off the Registrar's `owns` in `FACTORY_SERVICES` in the same task, or the TSK-093 file-map check fails on a claimed file that does not exist. `aws/codebuild.ts` stays split with the Landlord until the Landlord's extraction.
- Package `test` scripts list files explicitly; a new `*.test.ts` that is not added to the list never runs. A moved test that finds fixtures by a path relative to its own location (`import.meta.url`) must be checked after the move.
- A test that imports `app.ts` (`skill-checks.e2e.test.ts`, `skills.fetch.test.ts`) or `aws/codebuild.ts` (`skill-checks.test.ts`) stays in `control-plane`; only its import lines change.
- `skills.ts` loads the registry once, when first used, and serves it from memory (GAP-056). Keep that behavior when the store moves.
- `index.ts` listens at import and cannot be unit-tested there; wire it last.
- The Donna Test does not apply: this is Factory code (what is admitted, pinned and recorded), not an agent's behavior. Do not add interfaces beyond API, webhooks, WebSockets and events (`KPF.md`).

### Skills flow work stream (TSK-156 to TSK-162)

**Skills flow work stream (TSK-156 to TSK-162).** Intent: skills are registered with the factory and adopted by agents through configuration, not through a pull request on any repository. A skill is public or private; a private skill needs admin approval, then auto-adopts for its owner and no other agent. Each action in a skill is HITL or autonomous (E9). Adopting or revoking is a configuration change that rebuilds and redeploys the agent (SK3, SK4). How to work it: tasks are tagged `[Skills flow n/7]`; take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING` and whose `after` tasks are `COMPLETED`; follow §2 for each.

### Access flow work stream (TSK-171 to TSK-176)

**Access flow work stream (TSK-171 to TSK-176).** Intent: a person reaches an agent, and what it does for them, only through the roles they hold (E12). Roles grant people access; they never restrict an agent's own work, so scheduled and autonomous runs continue. A stranger's message is never delivered. The cartridge declares the roles (per skill), an admin assigns them to people in the console, the Bouncer admits a person who holds one, and the gatekeeper-egress enforces them on what the agent reaches. How to work it: tasks are tagged `[Access flow n/6]`; take the lowest `n` that is `PENDING`; follow §2 for each. **Order:** this work stream comes before the per-agent Skills tab (TSK-161) and the cartridge migration (TSK-162) (Dale, chat, 2026-10-10). **Scheduled work (TSK-177, GAP-131; Dale, chat, 2026-10-10):** scheduled work carries the authority of whoever requested it, never more. A schedule records `createdVia` (agent, console or api) and `requestedBy` (a person, a principal, or system); the factory fills `requestedBy` itself from the verified run or principal, so no caller or agent can name it. When the schedule fires, the factory re-resolves the requester's current roles and attaches a verified badge; if the requester no longer holds a role, the fire is skipped and ledgered. A `system` requester carries the agent's own authority. Existing schedules read as `system`.

**Intent.** The Landlord owns compute lifecycle: building and registering an agent's runtime, waking it from zero, the run states and their timeouts, warm-down, the operational pause and kill switches, the turn mailbox, and two-stage retirement (L1 to L6, §6.4). `packages/landlord` holds all of that behind one typed contract. The dependency points one way: the control plane, the Timekeeper (through its `fire` callback), the Inspector and the Keymaster depend on the Landlord; the Landlord depends on the Registrar (the agent record and the build callback) and the Bouncer (run privileges), and hosts the container runtime shim (`packages/hydrate`), never the reverse. The HTTP handlers for the wake and run routes stay in `control-plane`, because that is where the Bouncer's privileges and the Gatekeeper's run-token rule meet a request.

### Gatekeeper flow work stream (TSK-187 to TSK-194)

**Status (2026-10-12):** TSK-187 to TSK-194 are `COMPLETED`; the extraction is done (plan #150, code #152, #153, #154, #155, #158, #160 and the last PR). **Intent.** The Gatekeeper owns the perimeter: inbound presence and wakes, front-door authentication verification (A1, A2), the run-token rule, the policy data, and outbound proxying with credential injection (E1 to E6, S1). `packages/gatekeeper` holds all of that behind one typed contract. ## 6. Declared Architectural Intent (The Target State)

### 6.1 The "New Hire" Mental Model (Console vs. Cartridge)
An Agent Cartridge is modeled as a **Digital Employee** being onboarded into an enterprise:
1. **The Job Description & Instructions (`soul.md`):** Persona, responsibilities, tone, and strict behavioral boundaries.
2. **The Employment Contract & Access (`cartridge.yaml`):** Declared variable names for required secrets, trigger events, tool capabilities, and persistent memory prefix.
3. **The Toolbox (`skills` / MCP Tools):** External tools, APIs, or MCP servers placed at the agent's disposal. Skills are registered with the factory and adopted by the agent through its configuration (SK1–SK8): a private skill belongs to one agent and is adopted by it automatically once approved; a public skill is adopted by each agent that needs it, with approval. A skill is typically specific to one system, needing a route and a secret the factory holds, and contains actions, each held for a person or autonomous (SK2).
4. **The Filing Cabinet / Memory (`/memory`):** Embedded file-based databases (SQLite, DuckDB, JSON files) stored in `$MEMORY_DIR` that the Factory Console automatically hydrates on boot and syncs to object storage on sleep. (External shared enterprise databases are accessed via declared connection secrets).
5. **The Performance Rubric & Scorecard (`bench.yaml`):** Deterministic evaluation test cases ("When Y happens, do X; when B happens, do A") used to benchmark accuracy, latency, and token cost across models.
   - **Policy Gate Rule:** `bench.yaml` is **optional by default** at registration and across all stages. Factory administrators can configure company policy gates to require benchmarking at specific lifecycle milestones (registration, deployment, run, or production tier promotion).
6. **House staff (optional cartridges):** Agents the factory ships for the operator to admit, kept out of the ten platform services and out of the baked catalog (`agents/staff`, L2). The Personal Trainer trains a skill. Its training account is on the skill. Using the skill spends the adopting agent's budget. The trainer recommends a model and does not deploy (L5). See GAP-160.

### 6.2 The Unified `cartridge.yaml` Specification (Target Contract)
```yaml
schemaVersion: "1.0"
id: my-agent
name: "My Agent"
role: "Customer Operations"
prompt: "./soul.md"

triggers:
  - type: http
    path: /wake
  - type: webhook
    path: /hooks/events
    secretRef: WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET
  - type: cron
    schedule: "0 9 * * 1-5"
  - type: discord
    secretRef: DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN

secrets:
  requires:
    - name: SLACK_BOT_TOKEN
      description: "Slack bot OAuth token"
    - name: JIRA_API_KEY
      description: "API key for Jira issue management"

persistence:
  enabled: true
  prefix: "my-agent-state"

compute:
  kind: oci # or local, git
  ref: "ghcr.io/org/my-agent:latest"
  cpu: 256
  memory: 512
```

### 6.3 Invocation & Runtime Semantics
* **Zero Custom SDK Requirement:** The worker receives its invocation payload via standard input environment (`FACTORY_INPUT` / `/tmp/input.json`), or clean invocation handler, and outputs its result without needing bespoke REST polling loops.
* **Perimeter Defense:** The Cartridge never holds a public IP address or directly opens unauthenticated public ports. Everything that crosses the perimeter, in either direction, is the Gatekeeper's job (P1).
* **P1 – The Gatekeeper owns the perimeter.** It is two services. `gatekeeper-ingress` carries everything that reaches an agent: Discord presence, webhooks and other triggers, and the wakes they cause. `gatekeeper-egress` carries every outbound connection an agent makes (E1–E8): routes, hosts, models, metering and credential injection. The two deploy and scale separately, so a fault in one never cuts the other. Presence tells the truth: an agent shows available (online) only once its run is ready to take a turn, which is its first heartbeat or mailbox poll from inside the container; while a wake is in progress it shows starting, never available. No other component carries agent traffic across the perimeter.
* **P2 – One name for the perimeter.** `Gatekeeper`, `gatekeeper-ingress` and `gatekeeper-egress` are the only names the factory uses for its perimeter, in code, packages, images, services, infrastructure, configuration, environment variables, agent repositories and documentation. No alias, compatibility name or superseded identifier remains. Third-party proper names are not the factory's (for example Discord's Gateway API, a cloud NAT gateway). Only append-only history keeps what it recorded: ledger rows (§6.10) and §7 entries written before a rename.
* **Network & gatekeeper-egress Security:** See the egress invariants below. They apply to every provider and every cloud; there are **no exceptions**.

#### 6.3.1 Egress Invariants (each has a conformance test, §6.7)
* **E1 – Single egress path.** Every outbound connection from cartridge code goes through the gatekeeper-egress. No exceptions, including cloud-provider APIs. An agent's `NO_PROXY` may contain only loopback, `.internal` service discovery, and the link-local metadata/credential addresses.
* **E2 – Run attribution.** Every gatekeeper-egress request carries the run's short-lived token; requests without a valid, live run token are rejected.
* **E3 – Ledgered.** Every egress request or tunnel writes a ledger row (agent, run, route or host, allow/deny decision) — never prompt bodies or secrets.
* **E4 – Gated before forwarding.** The gatekeeper-egress enforces policy (routes, hosts, models), budget, pause/isolate/kill switch, and approvals before any byte leaves.
* **E5 – Every model call is metered.** Agents may use any model provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, AWS Bedrock, xAI, …), but every model call goes through a gatekeeper-egress **provider route** so tokens and cost are metered and ledgered. A cloud model service (e.g. Bedrock) is a gatekeeper-egress upstream configured by the landing zone; the gatekeeper-egress holds the cloud credentials, cartridges never do. A generic CONNECT tunnel is **not** an acceptable path for model calls, because it cannot meter tokens or cost.
* **E7 – Deny by default.** An agent with no admin-set policy has no egress. The control plane never grants routes, hosts, models, or budgets on its own (not at registration, not at run start). Only explicit admin actions change a policy: `PUT /api/v1/agents/:id/policy`, model approval, and the global policy. A cartridge's declared egress is a *request* shown to the admin, not a grant, and it bounds any grant (E8). Granting models is such an admin action: an agent with a policy of its own reaches the factory model API (the `models` route) without a separate route grant, and which models it gets is decided only by the policy's models (M2). The global fallback policy implies nothing, and provider-native routes (M4) stay explicit grants.
* **E8 – Declared is the ceiling.** An agent's repository declares everything it needs to do its job: every ingress trigger, every system it reaches and each action it takes there (method and path, E11), every host, model and tool (including each skill's actions, SK2), and every credential, including the ones the Gatekeeper injects for it. A policy may grant only what the admitted commit declares plus the requirements and actions of skills this agent has adopted at an approved version (SK3), and the control plane refuses a policy that grants more. A new ability means a change in the repo or an approved skill adoption, then admission and a redeploy of that configuration (SK4); an adoption is a configuration change and never a pull request. The Keymaster reports and accepts only declared credentials, and the Gatekeeper serves only declared, granted actions.
* **E9 – Acting as a person waits for that person.** A request an agent makes in a person's name that others will see (a post, a comment, a message) is held, not sent. Which actions are held is declared by the author of the skill that takes them in the skill's manifest (SK2), or, for an agent's own code, in its cartridge, per action (E11): `hold: true` is human-in-the-loop, `hold: false` is autonomous. An admin reviews that declaration when approving the skill version or admitting the agent and may change it, and may change any grant's hold at any time, effective on the next request. There is no factory default: an action is held only if its author declared it or an admin set it; the skill review shows every write action that is not held, for information. An agent can never change a hold. The gatekeeper-egress forwards nothing; the control plane keeps a reviewable copy of exactly what would be sent (never a credential, which the gatekeeper-egress injects only on release) and shows it to approvers. Approval releases that request once and unchanged: the agent sends the identical request again and the gatekeeper-egress forwards it; anything different is a new request and is held again. Rejection, with or without notes, sends nothing. The decision is delivered to the agent (its mailbox, or a new run), so a review can take as long as the person needs and no run waits for it. A held action's approval, and the identity of the person who gave it, is written to the ledger before the action is allowed, and a rejected one is ledgered too.
* **E10 – Systems are factory data.** Every external system an agent reaches, other than model providers (§6.9), is defined once in the factory: where requests go (upstream), the credential kind (static secret or OAuth connection) and how it is injected, and for OAuth the sign-in URL, token URL, token behaviour (refresh or expiry) and scopes, and the factory's own app registration with that system (its client secret held by the Keymaster, K1). An admin approves each definition once per factory, and it takes effect without a platform change or a redeploy. Agents and skills name a system; they never supply an upstream, a sign-in URL or an injection rule. Neither `agent-factory` nor any ops repository holds a list of systems.
* **E11 – Every agent–system pair is a grant.** A grant names the agent, the system, the actions it may take there (method and path, from the agent's admitted declaration and the approved skills it has adopted, E8, SK2, SK3), whether each action is held (E9), and the credential it uses: the agent's own, or a named shared one (K5.5). A policy owner approves every grant, shared system or not. The gatekeeper-egress forwards only granted actions and refuses and ledgers every other request to that system. It reads definitions and grants from the factory and caches them; a change applies to the next request without a redeploy.
* **E12 – A person reaches an agent, and what it does for them, only through the roles they hold.** Roles grant people access; they never restrict an agent's own work. (1) The cartridge declares its roles in `cartridge.yaml`: for each role, the skills it allows and the skills it denies (the agent's job descriptions). An admin reviews them when admitting the agent, as for holds (E9). A cartridge that declares no roles has none to assign, so only its owner, admins and operators reach it. (2) An admin assigns a person an agent role per agent, on the person's identity link (the Bouncer's data; the console edits it). `Owner` is never assigned: it comes from ownership. A role name the admitted cartridge does not declare is refused. (3) The Bouncer delivers a message to an agent only from a caller who holds a role on it: its owner, an admin or operator, or a person with an agent role there (the derived role `agent-member`, which may start runs and converse on that agent and do nothing else). Any other message is never delivered to the agent, and the refusal is ledgered. (4) For a run started by a person (its requester, attested by the ingress), the gatekeeper-egress forwards a call through a skill's route only if that person's roles allow the skill. It refuses and ledgers any other call before the credential is injected, and the agent cannot change the answer. A role allows a route when it allows a skill that declares that route and denies none that do (deny wins); a person whose roles allow nothing there gets nothing. Owners and admins are not restricted. A skill's routes are what it declares: a registered skill's `requires.routes` (SK2), a cartridge's own skill's `routes`. (5) A run with no requesting person (a schedule, a wake, internal work) is not restricted by this rule. (6) Roles are skill-based. The holds on a skill's actions (E9) are separate and unchanged: a role that allows a skill allows its autonomous actions, and a held action still waits for a person. (7) This stops an agent reaching a system for a person who may not. It does not stop an agent repeating what it already knows (its memory, §6.6): that is the cartridge's (the Donna Test).
* **E6 – Network-enforced.** Agent subnets have no internet or NAT route. Platform traffic that is not cartridge code (image pulls, logs, the shim's memory sync) may use private cloud endpoints.

#### 6.3.2 Secrets Invariant
* **S1 – Agents never hold real secrets.** A cartridge's declared secrets are delivered to it only as placeholders (its run token). The gatekeeper-egress injects the real credential into the matching outbound request, based on the route or host the secret is declared for. Secrets that must be used locally (key files for signing, form logins) get a gatekeeper-side exchange route instead, added per secret under a task; until a secret is converted it is listed in the Gap Register. As a backstop, the control plane and gatekeeper-egress redact every known secret value from results and ledger rows, for every runtime; the control plane caches secret values so this does not add a vault lookup per wake.

### 6.4 Two-Stage Agent Retirement Lifecycle (Zero Ongoing Cost Guarantee)
* **Objective:** Guarantee that retired agents incur strictly $0 in continuing cloud costs while providing safety against accidental operational destruction.
* **Stage 1: Soft-Retirement / Scream Test (`RETIRED_PENDING_PURGE`):**
  - All ingress triggers, gatekeeper-ingress presence gatekeeper-egress instances, and compute wakes are immediately cut. Compute drops to 0 replicas.
  - Secret bindings and persistent memory remain in place.
  - An administrative holding period (configurable, default: 1 calendar week) begins.
  - An operator can invoke `POST /api/v1/registry/agents/:id/reinstate` during this period to restore the agent to production.
* **Stage 2: Permanent Purge & Archival (`RETIRED`):**
  - Triggered after the holding window expires or via administrative override (`POST /api/v1/registry/agents/:id/purge`).
  - Cloud task definitions and compute services are deleted.
  - Secrets are permanently destroyed/purged from the enterprise vault.
  - Persistent mind storage is compressed and archived into cold tier object storage.
  - Immutable execution ledger rows remain permanently preserved.

### 6.5 Headless Observability & GraphQL Telemetry Surface
* **Strict Separation:** The Factory kernel never builds UI dashboards, formats PDF reports, or renders email summaries. It strictly captures structured data and serves it via headless APIs.
* **Core Metrics Pillars:** Cost (USD spend, token burn), Quantity (task throughput, turns, duration), Quality (deterministic benchmark pass rates, regression ledger).
* **Run Progress & Ingress Relays:** The Gatekeeper Egress emits metadata-only run progress events (`call.start`, `call.end`) with non-secret telemetry (route/model identifier, durationMs, status, outcome, inputTokens, outputTokens) without payload bodies or secrets (E3/S1). Control plane relays live run progress to Gatekeeper Ingress (`POST /api/v1/activity`) to drive channel presence and in-channel progress message updates.
* **API Surface Division:**
  - **REST:** Discrete operational actions (`/runs`, `/wake`, `/pause`, `/metrics`).
### 6.6 Agent Memory Architecture ("The Notebook & Safe")
* **Separation of Concerns:**
  - **Cartridge (The Employee):** Strictly reads and writes to local embedded files (SQLite) in `$MEMORY_DIR` (`<agent>_state.db`). Cartridges **never** import cloud SDKs (`boto3`, `@google-cloud/storage`, Azure Blob SDK) and never know about cloud storage buckets.
  - **The Building Safe (Property Manager):** Enforced by the Property Manager's container runtime shim (`packages/hydrate/src/shim.ts`). On wake, the shim pulls remote mind storage (`s3://...` / `gs://...`) into `$MEMORY_DIR`. On sleep/exit, the shim pushes `$MEMORY_DIR` back to the remote mind bucket.
* **SQLite Storage Engine Standards:**
  - **WAL Mode:** Every cartridge SQLite database must execute `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL;` and `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000;` on connection.
  - **Single-Writer Clean Checkpointing:** Cartridges execute `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` in their shutdown/exit cleanup (`finally:` block). This collapses the `-wal` and `-shm` write-ahead logs into the primary `.db` file, guaranteeing clean, atomic object storage synchronization without lock artifacts.
  - **Notebook Rotation (replaces rolling retention pruning, 2026-10-18):** conversation turns are not deleted; the notebook rotates instead (AM3).
* **Private Agent Memory Isolation:**
  - Cartridge memory is **strictly private** to each agent. Agents never share SQLite databases or object storage mind prefixes.
  - Cross-agent collaboration and knowledge sharing is strictly conducted via the gatekeeper-ingress (MCP) (`talk_to_agent`), preserving encapsulation, provenance, and auditability.
* **Architecture Specification:** See [`docs/architecture/agent-memory-models.md`](docs/architecture/agent-memory-models.md) for the complete specification covering the 3 Agent Archetypes, The Storage Triad, avoiding the "Decoder Ring" anti-pattern, and Multi-User Isolation.

* **AM1 – Every agent declares one of three archetypes (the user's decision, 2026-10-18).** `memory.archetype` in `cartridge.yaml` is required; the Registrar refuses a new agent version without it. Changing archetype is a commit and a redeploy.
  - **Purpose-Built:** answers questions for anyone. Follow-ups work within a live session (an in-memory rolling window); nothing persists after the session ends.
  - **Personal Assistant:** serves a primary user; others may engage it about that user. Keeps named conversations per user in its notebook.
  - **Team Member:** serves several users through its functions. Keeps private named conversations per user, plus shared project memory (open decision, GAP-180).
* **AM2 – Conversations belong to a user and an agent, never a channel.** The notebook is the agent's record; channels (Discord, console, Slack) are only routes. Each turn is recorded with its channel, and replies go back out on the channel the turn came from. Every turn the factory delivers carries the person's stable identity and where to send the reply.
* **AM3 – Notebooks rotate; nothing is forgotten.** When the current notebook reaches 10 MB or 90 days, whichever comes first (factory-wide policy), the agent closes it as a read-only volume and starts a new one, carrying forward lasting knowledge, open conversations and an index of the closed volumes. The factory keeps closed volumes in cold storage, hydrates only the current notebook on wake, and returns an archived volume when the agent asks for it.
* **AM4 – Named conversations.** For Personal Assistant and Team Member agents, the user or the agent can name a conversation. Asking the agent to find one searches the current notebook and the index of closed volumes; resuming one brings its recent turns back into the agent's context and records new turns against that conversation.
* **AM5 – The console reads conversations through the agent.** The factory defines a request every template agent answers: list a user's conversations, or return a conversation's last several turns. The console uses it to resume a conversation and show its recent turns, from any channel. The factory never opens a notebook itself. Any admin read of another user's conversation is recorded in the ledger.
* **Open decision – shared project conversations (GAP-180).** How a Team Member agent records and reads conversations several people share (GitHub issues, Jira tickets, Discord threads) is not yet decided, including whether every channel is mirrored the same way. Nothing is built against it until the user decides.

### 6.7 Enforcement (This Document Is Checked by Machines)
* Every machine-checkable invariant in this document names its conformance test; the tests live in `packages/conformance` and run in CI.
* CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) must be green to merge to `main`, enforced by GitHub branch protection. A skipped test suite counts as a failure unless it is listed in the Gap Register with an owning task.
* AI sessions run the conformance tests before any `git commit` or `git push` (Claude Code hook in `.claude/settings.json`); a failure blocks the commit.
* Deploys ship only commits on `main` with green CI, tagged by commit SHA (never `:latest`). A deploy's definition of done includes one model call proven, by its ledger row, to have gone through a gatekeeper-egress provider route.


#### Invariant coverage (machine-checked)

Every invariant in §6 has a row. `checked`: its tests would catch a violation. `partial`: its tests cover only what the Scope column says. `unchecked`: nothing tests it. A `partial` or `unchecked` row names the gap that owns closing it. `packages/conformance/src/coverage.test.ts` fails CI if an invariant has no row, a row names a test that does not exist or does not cite the invariant in a `describe`/`it` title, or a row that is not `checked` has no registered gap.
